**Lemmas and theorems.**

**Lemma P (strong measurability and finite integral of a dominated limit).** Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be a measure space.

1. Let $\beta$ be a pseudo-metrizable topological space, let $u$ be a non-bottom countably generated filter on $\iota$ (`[NeBot u] [IsCountablyGenerated u]`), and let $F : \iota \to \alpha \to \beta$, $f : \alpha \to \beta$ satisfy `∀ i, AEStronglyMeasurable (F i) μ` and `∀ᵐ x ∂μ, Tendsto (fun i => F i x) u (𝓝 (f x))`. Then `AEStronglyMeasurable f μ` (`aestronglyMeasurable_of_tendsto_ae`; everywhere form `stronglyMeasurable_of_tendsto`).
2. Let $E$ be a normed additive commutative group, and let $F : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$, $f : \alpha \to E$, and $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$ satisfy `HasFiniteIntegral bound μ`, `∀ n, ∀ᵐ a ∂μ, ‖F n a‖ ≤ bound a`, and `∀ᵐ a ∂μ, Tendsto (fun n => F n a) atTop (𝓝 (f a))`. Then $\lVert f(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ for $\mu$-a.e. $a$, and `HasFiniteIntegral f μ` (`ae_norm_ofReal_f_le_bound`, `hasFiniteIntegral_of_dominated_convergence`). [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/AEStronglyMeasurable.lean:693–708`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/AEStronglyMeasurable.lean#L693-L708) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/Basic.lean:784–801`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/Basic.lean#L784-L801) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/L1Space/HasFiniteIntegral.lean:319–381`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/L1Space/HasFiniteIntegral.lean#L319-L381)

**Lemma Q (lower-integral dominated convergence via decreasing tail suprema, and $L^1$-norm convergence).**

1. Let $H : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to [0, \infty]$ and $h, b : \alpha \to [0, \infty]$ satisfy `∀ n, AEMeasurable (H n) μ`, `∀ n, H n ≤ᵐ[μ] b`, $\int^- a,\,b(a)\,d\mu \neq \infty$, and `∀ᵐ a ∂μ, Tendsto (fun n => H n a) atTop (𝓝 (h a))`. Then
   $$\limsup_{n \to \infty} \int^- a,\,H_n(a)\,d\mu \le \int^- a,\,\limsup_{n \to \infty} H_n(a)\,d\mu \qquad (\text{when each $H_n$ is measurable}),$$
   and $\int^- a,\,H_n(a)\,d\mu \to \int^- a,\,h(a)\,d\mu$ as $n \to \infty$ (`limsup_lintegral_le`, `tendsto_lintegral_of_dominated_convergence`, `tendsto_lintegral_of_dominated_convergence'`).
2. Let $E$ be a normed additive commutative group, and let $F : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$, $f : \alpha \to E$, and $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$ satisfy `∀ n, AEStronglyMeasurable (F n) μ`, `HasFiniteIntegral bound μ`, `∀ n, ∀ᵐ a ∂μ, ‖F n a‖ ≤ bound a`, and `∀ᵐ a ∂μ, Tendsto (fun n => F n a) atTop (𝓝 (f a))`. Then
   $$\lim_{n \to \infty} \int^- a,\,\mathrm{ENNReal.ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a) - f(a)\rVert\,d\mu = 0$$
   (`tendsto_lintegral_norm_of_dominated_convergence`). [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/DominatedConvergence.lean:27–78`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/DominatedConvergence.lean#L27-L78) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/L1Space/HasFiniteIntegral.lean:383–431`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/L1Space/HasFiniteIntegral.lean#L383-L431)

The continuity step in Lemma R uses the [extension of the simple-class operator](#banach-dense-extension).

**Lemma R (dominated convergence for `setToFun`, sequential and filter forms).** Let $E, F$ be normed $\mathbb R$-spaces and let $hT : \mathrm{DominatedFinMeasAdditive}\,\mu\,T\,C$.

1. *Sequential form (`tendsto_setToFun_of_dominated_convergence`).* If $F_n : \alpha \to E$ ($n \in \mathbb N$) is $\mu$-a.e. strongly measurable for every $n$, $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$ is $\mu$-integrable, $\lVert F_n(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ $\mu$-a.e. for every $n$, and $F_n(a) \to f(a)$ $\mu$-a.e. as $n \to \infty$, then $\mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,(F_n) \to \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,f$.
2. *Countably-generated-filter form (`tendsto_setToFun_filter_of_dominated_convergence`).* Let $l$ be a countably generated filter on $\iota$. If $\{n \in \iota \mid \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(F_n)\,\mu\} \in l$, $\{n \in \iota \mid \forall^{\mathrm{ae}} a,\ \lVert F_n(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)\} \in l$, $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$ is $\mu$-integrable, and $\forall^{\mathrm{ae}} a,\ F_n(a) \to f(a)$ along $l$, then $\mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,(F_n) \to \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,f$ along $l$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean:1252–1317`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean#L1252-L1317)

**Theorem S (Bochner dominated convergence and its signed-truncation consumers).** Let $G$ be a normed $\mathbb R$-vector space (`[NormedAddCommGroup G] [NormedSpace ℝ G]`).

1. *Sequence form (`tendsto_integral_of_dominated_convergence`).* Under the hypotheses of Lemma R(1) for $F : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to G$, $f : \alpha \to G$, and $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$, we have $\int a,\,F_n(a)\,d\mu \to \int a,\,f(a)\,d\mu$ as $n \to \infty$.
2. *Filter form (`tendsto_integral_filter_of_dominated_convergence`).* Under the hypotheses of Lemma R(2) for `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]` and $F : \iota \to \alpha \to G$, we have $\int a,\,F_n(a)\,d\mu \to \int a,\,f(a)\,d\mu$ along $l$.
3. *Payment to the signed truncation and Euler-limit consumers.*
   - Given an a.e. cover $\varphi : \iota \to \mathcal P(\alpha)$ of $\mu$ along a countably generated filter $l$ and an integrable $f : \alpha \to G$, applying (2) to $F_i = 1_{\varphi_i} f$ with $\mathrm{bound}(a) = \lVert f(a)\rVert$ yields $\int_{\varphi_i} f\,d\mu \to \int f\,d\mu$ (`AECover.integral_tendsto_of_countably_generated`), which directly discharges `intervalIntegral_tendsto_integral_Ioi` (§2.3.2.4, Theorem D value step; §2.3.2.6, Theorem N(3); `GIntegral.l21a`) and `tendsto_partialGamma` (§2.3.2.3, §2.3.2.6).
   - For $s \in \mathbb C$ with $\Re s > 0$, applying (1) on $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol}|_{(0,\infty)})$ to $F_n(x) = 1_{(0, n]}(x)(1 - x/n)^n x^{s-1}$ with $\mathrm{bound}(x) = e^{-x} x^{\Re s - 1}$ (via $(1 - x/n)^n \le e^{-x}$ on $(0, n]$) yields $\int_0^n (1 - x/n)^n x^{s-1}\,dx \to \Gamma(s)$ (`Complex.approx_Gamma_integral_tendsto_Gamma_integral`, consumed by Euler's limit formula in §2.3.2.1). [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/DominatedConvergence.lean:57–74`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/DominatedConvergence.lean#L57-L74) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/IntegralEqImproper.lean:481–489,658–664`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/IntegralEqImproper.lean#L481-L489) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/Beta.lean:273–333`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/Beta.lean#L273-L333)

**Corollary T (parametric differentiation and local uniform domination of the complex Mellin and Gamma integrals).** Let $\mathbb K \in \{\mathbb R, \mathbb C\}$ and let $E$ be a normed space over $\mathbb R$ and $\mathbb K$.

1. *Differentiation under the integral sign (`hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_deriv_le`).* Let $s \in \mathcal N(x_0)$ in $\mathbb K$, let $F : \mathbb K \to \alpha \to E$ and $F' : \mathbb K \to \alpha \to E$ satisfy `∀ᶠ x in 𝓝 x₀, AEStronglyMeasurable (F x) μ`, `Integrable (F x₀) μ`, and `AEStronglyMeasurable (F' x₀) μ`, and suppose there is a $\mu$-integrable $\mathrm{bound} : \alpha \to \mathbb R$ such that for $\mu$-a.e. $a$, every $x \in s$ satisfies $\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,(F(\cdot, a))\,(F'(x, a))\,x$ and $\lVert F'(x, a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$. Then $F'(x_0, \cdot)$ is $\mu$-integrable and $x \mapsto \int a,\,F(x, a)\,d\mu$ has $\mathbb K$-derivative $\int a,\,F'(x_0, a)\,d\mu$ at $x_0$.
2. *Local uniform domination for Mellin and Gamma (`mellin_hasDerivAt_of_isBigO_rpow`, `hasDerivAt_GammaIntegral`, `Complex.differentiableAt_Gamma`, `Complex.continuousAt_Gamma`).* If $f : \mathbb R \to E$ (for a Banach $\mathbb C$-space $E$) is locally integrable on $(0, \infty)$ with $f(t) = O(t^{-a})$ as $t \to \infty$ and $f(t) = O(t^{-b})$ as $t \to 0^+$, then at every $s_0 \in \mathbb C$ with $b < \Re s_0 < a$, choosing $0 < v < \min(\Re s_0 - b, a - \Re s_0)$ makes the two-end envelope
   $$\mathrm{bound}(t) := \bigl(t^{\Re s_0 + v - 1} + t^{\Re s_0 - v - 1}\bigr)\,\lvert\log t\rvert\,\lVert f(t)\rVert$$
   integrable on $(0, \infty)$ and uniformly above $\lVert t^{z-1}(\log t)f(t)\rVert$ for all $z \in B(s_0, v)$. Consequently $\operatorname{mellin} f$ has complex derivative $\operatorname{mellin}(t \mapsto (\log t)\,f(t))(s_0)$ at $s_0$. Specializing to $f(t) = e^{-t}$ ($b = 0$, $a = \Re s_0 + 1$) gives $\Gamma'(s_0) = \int_0^\infty t^{s_0 - 1}(\log t)e^{-t}\,dt$ for $\Re s_0 > 0$, and functional-equation induction extends complex differentiability and continuity of $\Gamma$ to $\mathbb C \setminus \{0, -1, -2, \dots\}$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/ParametricIntegral.lean:78–179,262–306`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/ParametricIntegral.lean#L78-L179) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/MellinTransform.lean:320–407`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/MellinTransform.lean#L320-L407) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/Deriv.lean:45–95`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/Deriv.lean#L45-L95)

---

**Proofs.**

*Proof of Lemma P.* For (1), [§2.3.2.8](#metric-valued-ae-limits) gives the measurable patch and the proof of the effective range. Equip $\beta$ with its Borel $\sigma$-algebra (`borelize β`). By the characterization `aestronglyMeasurable_iff_aemeasurable_separable` (retained at `[Lean]`), a map into a pseudo-metrizable space is $\mu$-a.e. strongly measurable if and only if it is Borel $\mu$-a.e. measurable and takes values $\mu$-a.e. in a separable subset of $\beta$. Borel $\mu$-a.e. measurability of $f$ follows from `aemeasurable_of_tendsto_metrizable_ae` ([§2.3.2.8](#metric-valued-ae-limits), Theorem V). For separability, because $u$ is non-bottom and countably generated, `u.exists_seq_tendsto` selects one sequence $v : \mathbb N \to \iota$ tending to $u$. For each $n \in \mathbb N$, `AEStronglyMeasurable (F (v n)) μ` supplies a separable set $t_n \subset \beta$ such that $A_n := \{x \in \alpha \mid F_{v(n)}(x) \in t_n\}$ has full $\mu$-measure. By countable intersection of full-measure sets (`ae_all_iff`), $\bigcap_{n \in \mathbb N} A_n$ has full $\mu$-measure, and intersecting it with the full-measure set on which $F_i(x) \to f(x)$ along $u$ gives a full-measure set $A_\infty$. For every $x \in A_\infty$, composition with $v \to u$ yields $F_{v(n)}(x) \to f(x)$ as $n \to \infty$ with $F_{v(n)}(x) \in t_n \subset \bigcup_{k \in \mathbb N} t_k$, so $f(x)$ belongs to the closure $\overline{\bigcup_{k \in \mathbb N} t_k}$. A countable union of separable sets is separable and closure preserves separability (`IsSeparable.iUnion`, `IsSeparable.closure`, read in [§2.3.2.8](#metric-valued-ae-limits)), establishing `AEStronglyMeasurable f μ`.

For (2), applying the monotone continuous map $\mathrm{ENNReal.ofReal} : \mathbb R \to [0, \infty]$ to $\lVert F_n(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ gives $\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))$ $\mu$-a.e. for each $n \in \mathbb N$ (`all_ae_norm_ofReal_F_le_bound`). Because the index set $\mathbb N$ is countable, `ae_all_iff` collects these into a *single* full-measure set on which $\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))$ holds for all $n$ simultaneously. On the intersection with the full-measure set where $F_n(a) \to f(a)$, continuity of the norm and of `ofReal` gives $\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a)\rVert \to \mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert f(a)\rVert$, and order-closedness of $[0, \infty]$ (`le_of_tendsto'`) yields $\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert f(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))$ $\mu$-a.e. (`ae_norm_ofReal_f_le_bound`). Monotonicity of the lower integral then gives
$$\int^- a,\,\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert f(a)\rVert\,d\mu \le \int^- a,\,\mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))\,d\mu.$$
At $n = 0$, $0 \le \lVert F_0(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ shows $\mathrm{bound} \ge 0$ $\mu$-a.e., so `hasFiniteIntegral_iff_ofReal` identifies $\int^- a,\,\mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))\,d\mu < \infty$ with `HasFiniteIntegral bound μ`. Hence `HasFiniteIntegral f μ` holds. $\square$

*Proof of Lemma Q.* For (1), assume first that every $H_n$ is genuinely measurable (`Measurable (H n)`). Define for each $n \in \mathbb N$ the tail-supremum function
$$U_n(a) := \sup_{i \ge n} H_i(a) \in [0, \infty].$$
Each $U_n$ is measurable as a countable supremum of measurable functions, and $U_0 \ge U_1 \ge U_2 \ge \cdots$ is pointwise nonincreasing (`Antitone`). Collecting the countable family of bounds $H_i \le^{\mathrm{ae}} b$ via `ae_all_iff` gives $U_0 \le^{\mathrm{ae}} b$, so $\int^- U_0\,d\mu \le \int^- b\,d\mu < \infty$. For every $n$ and every $i \ge n$, $H_i \le U_n$ gives $\int^- H_i\,d\mu \le \int^- U_n\,d\mu$, hence $\sup_{i \ge n} \int^- H_i\,d\mu \le \int^- U_n\,d\mu$ (`iSup₂_lintegral_le`). Taking the infimum over $n \in \mathbb N$ and applying the decreasing monotone convergence theorem `lintegral_iInf` (§2.3.2.6, Lemma M, which is valid because $\int^- U_0\,d\mu < \infty$ allows subtracting $U_n$ from $U_0$ and invoking upward monotone convergence `lintegral_iSup_ae`) gives `limsup_lintegral_le`:
$$\limsup_{n \to \infty} \int^- H_n\,d\mu = \inf_{n \in \mathbb N} \sup_{i \ge n} \int^- H_i\,d\mu \le \inf_{n \in \mathbb N} \int^- U_n\,d\mu = \int^- \left(\inf_{n \in \mathbb N} U_n\right) d\mu = \int^- \left(\limsup_{n \to \infty} H_n\right) d\mu.$$
On the other hand, Fatou's lemma `lintegral_liminf_le` (§2.3.2.6, derived directly from upward monotone convergence `lintegral_iSup'` applied to $n \mapsto \inf_{i \ge n} H_i$) gives the complementary lower inequality
$$\int^- h\,d\mu = \int^- \left(\liminf_{n \to \infty} H_n\right) d\mu \le \liminf_{n \to \infty} \int^- H_n\,d\mu.$$
Since $\limsup_{n \to \infty} H_n = h$ $\mu$-a.e., combining the two inequalities yields $\int^- h\,d\mu \le \liminf \int^- H_n\,d\mu \le \limsup \int^- H_n\,d\mu \le \int^- h\,d\mu$, so $\int^- H_n\,d\mu \to \int^- h\,d\mu$ in $[0, \infty]$ (`tendsto_of_le_liminf_of_limsup_le`, `Lebesgue/DominatedConvergence.lean:50–58`). When each $H_n$ is only `AEMeasurable`, `tendsto_lintegral_of_dominated_convergence'` (`:61–78`) replaces $H_n$ by its measurable representative $\widetilde H_n := (h_{H_n}).\mathrm{mk}\,H_n$; `lintegral_congr_ae` gives $\int^- H_n\,d\mu = \int^- \widetilde H_n\,d\mu$, and `ae_all_iff` ensures $\widetilde H_n(a) = H_n(a)$ for all $n \in \mathbb N$ simultaneously on one full-measure set, transferring both $\widetilde H_n \le^{\mathrm{ae}} b$ and $\widetilde H_n \to h$ $\mu$-a.e. to the measurable theorem.

For (2), Lemma P(1) gives `AEStronglyMeasurable f μ`, and Lemma P(2) gives $\mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert f(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))$ $\mu$-a.e. Set $H_n(a) := \mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a) - f(a)\rVert$ and $b(a) := 2\,\mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a))$. Each $H_n$ is `AEMeasurable` because $F_n - f$ is $\mu$-a.e. strongly measurable and both the norm and `ofReal` are continuous. By the triangle inequality $\lVert F_n(a) - f(a)\rVert \le \lVert F_n(a)\rVert + \lVert f(a)\rVert$ and additivity of `ofReal` on $[0, \infty)$,
$$H_n(a) \le \mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a)\rVert + \mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert f(a)\rVert \le 2\,\mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound}(a)) = b(a) \quad \text{($\mu$-a.e.)}$$
for every $n \in \mathbb N$ (`HasFiniteIntegral.lean:394–406`). Since $\mathrm{bound} \ge 0$ $\mu$-a.e. (from $n = 0$) and `HasFiniteIntegral bound μ` holds, $\int^- b\,d\mu = 2\int^- \mathrm{ofReal}(\mathrm{bound})\,d\mu < \infty$ (`:422–428`). Finally, $F_n(a) \to f(a)$ $\mu$-a.e. implies $\lVert F_n(a) - f(a)\rVert \to 0$ and hence $H_n(a) \to \mathrm{ofReal}\,0 = 0$ $\mu$-a.e. (`:408–411`). Applying part (1) with $h \equiv 0$ and rewriting $\int^- 0\,d\mu = 0$ gives $\int^- H_n\,d\mu \to 0$. $\square$

*Proof of Lemma R.* For (1), `tendsto_setToFun_of_dominated_convergence` (`SetToL1.lean:1252–1294`) first branches by classical logic on `CompleteSpace F`. If `¬CompleteSpace F`, `setToFun` evaluates to $0 \in F$ on every input by definition (`:630`), so the constant sequence $0$ tends to $0$ (`:1258–1259`). Assume now `[CompleteSpace F]`. Lemma P(1) gives `f_measurable : AEStronglyMeasurable f μ` (`:1261–1262`). For each $n$, `bound_integrable.mono'` combines `AEStronglyMeasurable (F n) μ` and $\lVert F_n\rVert \le^{\mathrm{ae}} \mathrm{bound}$ to give $h_n : \mathrm{Integrable}\,(F_n)\,\mu$ (`fs_int n`, `:1264–1265`), while pairing `f_measurable` with Lemma P(2) gives $h_f : \mathrm{Integrable}\,f\,\mu$ (`f_int`, `:1266–1269`). On integrable functions over a complete target, `setToFun_eq` rewrites
$$\mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,(F_n) = \mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\,(h_n.\mathrm{toL1}\,F_n), \qquad \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,f = \mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\,(h_f.\mathrm{toL1}\,f)$$
(`:1271–1276`). Because `L1.setToL1 hT` is a continuous linear map on $\alpha \to_1[\mu] E$, `L1.tendsto_setToL1` reduces the goal to $h_n.\mathrm{toL1}\,F_n \to h_f.\mathrm{toL1}\,f$ in $\alpha \to_1[\mu] E$, equivalently $\lVert h_n.\mathrm{toL1}\,F_n - h_f.\mathrm{toL1}\,f\rVert \to 0$ (`:1278–1280`). By linearity of `toL1` (`Integrable.toL1_sub`), the almost-everywhere representative congruence `coeFn_toL1`, and the $L^1$ norm formula `L1.norm_def` (all retained at `[Lean]`), for every $n$,
$$\bigl\lVert h_n.\mathrm{toL1}\,F_n - h_f.\mathrm{toL1}\,f\bigr\rVert = \mathrm{ENNReal.toReal}\!\left(\int^- a,\,\mathrm{ENNReal.ofReal}\,\lVert F_n(a) - f(a)\rVert\,d\mu\right)$$
(`:1286–1293`). Lemma Q(2) sends the lower integral inside `toReal` to $0$ in $[0, \infty]$, and continuity of `ENNReal.toReal` at $0 \neq \infty$ (`tendsto_toReal zero_ne_top`) sends the norm to $0 \in \mathbb R$ (`:1281–1285`).

For (2), `tendsto_setToFun_filter_of_dominated_convergence` (`SetToL1.lean:1296–1317`) uses `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]` to rewrite convergence of $n \mapsto \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,(F_n)$ along $l$ via the sequential criterion `tendsto_iff_seq_tendsto` (retained at `[Lean]`): it suffices to show that for every sequence $x : \mathbb N \to \iota$ tending to $l$ along `atTop`, the composite sequence $b \mapsto \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,(F_{x(b)})$ tends to $\mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T\,hT\,f$ (`:1302–1303`). Because filters are closed under binary intersection (`inter_mem`), the set
$$S := \bigl\{i \in \iota \mid \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(F_i)\,\mu\bigr\} \cap \bigl\{i \in \iota \mid \forall^{\mathrm{ae}} a,\ \lVert F_i(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)\bigr\}$$
belongs to $l$ (`:1305–1308`). Since $x \to l$ along `atTop`, there exists a threshold $k \in \mathbb N$ such that $x(b) \in S$ for all $b \ge k$ (`:1304, 1309`). Shifting indices by $k$ does not alter convergence along `atTop` (`tendsto_add_atTop_iff_nat k`, `:1310`), so it suffices to check that the shifted $\mathbb N$-sequence $n \mapsto F_{x(n+k)}$ satisfies the hypotheses of part (1):

- for every $n \in \mathbb N$, $n + k \ge k$ gives $x(n + k) \in S$, which supplies both `AEStronglyMeasurable (F (x (n + k))) μ` (`:1312`) and $\forall^{\mathrm{ae}} a,\ \lVert F_{x(n+k)}(a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ (`:1313`);
- on the single full-measure set given by `h_lim` where $i \mapsto F_i(a)$ tends to $f(a)$ along $l$, composing with $n \mapsto x(n + k)$ (which tends to $l$ along `atTop`) gives $F_{x(n+k)}(a) \to f(a)$ as $n \to \infty$ (`:1314–1316`).

Applying part (1) completes the proof. $\square$

*Proof of Theorem S.* Parts (1) and (2) rewrite $\int a,\,g(a)\,d\mu = \mathrm{setToFun}\,\mu\,T_\mu\,h_{T_\mu}\,g$ on both sides via `integral_eq_setToFun` (for `weightedSMul μ` and `dominatedFinMeasAdditive_weightedSMul μ`) and invoke Lemma R(1) and Lemma R(2) directly (`DominatedConvergence.lean:62–64, 72–74`).

For (3), let $\varphi : \iota \to \mathcal P(\alpha)$ be an `AECover μ l` with `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]` and let $f : \alpha \to G$ be $\mu$-integrable (`IntegralEqImproper.lean:481–489`). For each $i$, $\int_{\varphi_i} f\,d\mu = \int 1_{\varphi_i} f\,d\mu$ hold eventually along $l$ because $\varphi_i$ is eventually measurable (`hφ.measurable`, `integral_indicator`). Moreover: (i) $1_{\varphi_i} f$ is eventually $\mu$-a.e. strongly measurable because $f$ is (`AECover.aestronglyMeasurable`); (ii) at every point $x \in \alpha$, $\lVert (1_{\varphi_i} f)(x)\rVert \le \lVert f(x)\rVert$, and $\lVert f\rVert$ is $\mu$-integrable (`hfi.norm`); and (iii) $\mu$-almost everywhere, $x$ eventually lies in $\varphi_i$, so $(1_{\varphi_i} f)(x) \to f(x)$ along $l$ (`hφ.ae_tendsto_indicator`). Applying part (2) with $\mathrm{bound}(x) = \lVert f(x)\rVert$ yields $\int_{\varphi_i} f\,d\mu \to \int f\,d\mu$, closing the deferred call in §2.3.2.4 (Theorem D) and §2.3.2.6 (Theorem N(3), `intervalIntegral_tendsto_integral_Ioi`, `tendsto_partialGamma`, and `GIntegral.l21a`). For Euler's product sequence in `Gamma/Beta.lean:273–333`, on $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol}|_{(0,\infty)})$ each $F_n(x) = 1_{(0, n]}(x)(1 - x/n)^n x^{s-1}$ is integrable on $(0, n]$ (`intervalIntegrable_cpow'` times the continuous polynomial $(1 - x/n)^n$), hence $\mathrm{vol}|_{(0,\infty)}$-a.e. strongly measurable; for every $x > 0$, once $n \ge \lceil x\rceil$, $F_n(x) = (1 - x/n)^n x^{s-1} \to e^{-x} x^{s-1}$; and on $(0, n]$ the elementary convexity bound $0 \le (1 - x/n)^n \le e^{-x}$ (`one_sub_div_pow_le_exp_neg`) together with $\lvert x^{s-1}\rvert = x^{\Re s - 1}$ gives $\lvert F_n(x)\rvert \le e^{-x} x^{\Re s - 1}$, which is integrable on $(0, \infty)$ for $\Re s > 0$ (`Real.GammaIntegral_convergent`, §2.3.2.3). Applying part (1) gives $\int_0^n (1 - x/n)^n x^{s-1}\,dx \to \int_0^\infty e^{-x} x^{s-1}\,dx = \Gamma(s)$. $\square$

*Proof of Corollary T.* For (1), choose $\varepsilon > 0$ with $B(x_0, \varepsilon) \subseteq s$ (`ParametricIntegral.lean:294`). On the convex ball $B(x_0, \varepsilon)$, the mean-value theorem (`Convex.lipschitzOnWith_of_nnnorm_hasDerivWithin_le`, retained at `[Lean]`) turns the uniform derivative bound $\forall x \in B(x_0, \varepsilon),\ \lVert F'(x, a)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(a)$ into the uniform Lipschitz bound $\lVert F(x, a) - F(x_0, a)\rVert \le \lvert\mathrm{bound}(a)\rvert\,\lVert x - x_0\rVert$ for $\mu$-a.e. $a$ (`:298–304`), reducing `hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_deriv_le` through `hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_lip` (`:262–283`) to `hasFDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_lip'` (`:78–159`). There, let $b(a) := \lvert\mathrm{bound}(a)\rvert \in L^1(\mu)$, and let $L(a) \in \mathbb K \to_L E$ denote scalar multiplication by $F'(x_0, a)$. First, for every $x \in B(x_0, \varepsilon)$, $\lVert F(x, a) - F(x_0, a)\rVert \le \varepsilon\,b(a)$ $\mu$-a.e. makes $F(x, \cdot)$ integrable (`integrable_of_norm_sub_le`, `:94–102`), while sending $x \to x_0$ in the Lipschitz bound gives $\lVert L(a)\rVert \le b(a)$ $\mu$-a.e. and hence `Integrable (F' x₀) μ` (`:103–108`). After discarding the trivial `¬CompleteSpace E` branch (`:111–118`), linearity of the Bochner integral rewrites the Fréchet remainder quotient for $x \in B(x_0, \varepsilon)$ as
$$\lVert x - x_0\rVert^{-1}\left\lVert \int a,\,F(x, a)\,d\mu - \int a,\,F(x_0, a)\,d\mu - (x - x_0)\cdot \int a,\,F'(x_0, a)\,d\mu\right\rVert = \left\lVert \int a,\,Q(x, a)\,d\mu\right\rVert,$$
where $Q(x, a) := \lVert x - x_0\rVert^{-1}\bigl(F(x, a) - F(x_0, a) - (x - x_0)\,F'(x_0, a)\bigr)$ (`:120–127`). Along the countably generated neighbourhood filter `𝓝 x₀` (since $\mathbb K \in \{\mathbb R, \mathbb C\}$ is first countable):

- $Q(x, \cdot)$ is eventually $\mu$-a.e. strongly measurable (`:131–134`);
- for every $x \in B(x_0, \varepsilon)$ and $\mu$-a.e. $a$, the triangle inequality gives
  $$\lVert Q(x, a)\rVert \le \lVert x - x_0\rVert^{-1}\lVert F(x, a) - F(x_0, a)\rVert + \lVert F'(x_0, a)\rVert \le b(a) + \lVert F'(x_0, a)\rVert,$$
  which is $\mu$-integrable and independent of $x$ (`:135–150`);
- for $\mu$-a.e. $a$, differentiability of $F(\cdot, a)$ at $x_0$ means exactly $Q(x, a) \to 0$ as $x \to x_0$ (`:151–159`).

Applying Theorem S(2) along `𝓝 x₀` gives $\int a,\,Q(x, a)\,d\mu \to 0$, proving `HasDerivAt` at $x_0$ (`:128–130`).

For (2), in `mellin_hasDerivAt_of_isBigO_rpow` (`MellinTransform.lean:320–396`), let $F(z, t) = t^{z-1} f(t)$ and $F'(z, t) = t^{z-1}(\log t)f(t)$ on $(0, \infty)$. Since $b < \Re s_0 < a$, pick $v > 0$ with $b < \Re s_0 - v$ and $\Re s_0 + v < a$ (`:328–332`). For every $t > 0$ and $z \in B(s_0, v)$, $\lvert z - s_0\rvert < v$ implies $\Re s_0 - v < \Re z < \Re s_0 + v$: if $t \ge 1$, then $t^{\Re z - 1} \le t^{\Re s_0 + v - 1} \le t^{\Re s_0 + v - 1} + t^{\Re s_0 - v - 1}$; if $0 < t < 1$, then $t^{\Re z - 1} \le t^{\Re s_0 - v - 1} \le t^{\Re s_0 + v - 1} + t^{\Re s_0 - v - 1}$ (`:348–366`). Multiplying by $\lvert\log t\rvert\,\lVert f(t)\rVert$ bounds $\lVert F'(z, t)\rVert \le \mathrm{bound}(t)$ uniformly on $B(s_0, v)$. Because $\log t = o(t^\delta)$ as $t \to \infty$ and $\log t = o(t^{-\delta})$ as $t \to 0^+$ for every $\delta > 0$ (`isLittleO_log_rpow_atTop`, retained at `[Lean]`), choosing intermediate exponents in $(b, \Re s_0 - v)$ and $(\Re s_0 + v, a)$ absorbs $\lvert\log t\rvert$ into the two power-decay hypotheses and proves `IntegrableOn bound (Ioi 0)` (`:367–385`). Since $\partial_z(t^{z-1}) = t^{z-1}\log t$ on $t > 0$ (`HasDerivAt.const_cpow`, `:386–393`), part (1) applies on $s = B(s_0, v) \in \mathcal N(s_0)$ (`:394–396`). Specializing to $f(t) = e^{-t}$ (`Gamma/Deriv.lean:45–62`), $e^{-t} = O(t^{-(\Re s_0 + 1)})$ at $+\infty$ and $e^{-t} = O(1) = O(t^0)$ at $0^+$, giving `hasDerivAt_GammaIntegral` on $\{\Re s > 0\}$. Since $\{\Re s > 0\}$ is open and $\Gamma(s) = \mathrm{GammaIntegral}(s)$ there (`Gamma_eq_integral`, §2.3.2.3), $\Gamma$ is complex-differentiable on $\{\Re s > 0\}$; `differentiableAt_Gamma` (`:65–83`) then inducts on $n \in \mathbb N$ over $\{s \in \mathbb C \mid \Re s > -n\}$ via $\Gamma(s) = \Gamma(s + 1)/s$ away from $s = -m$ ($m \in \mathbb N$), and `continuousAt_Gamma` (`:88–89`) follows immediately. $\square$

---

**Remark (sharpness and hypothesis-deletion counterexamples).** [Expository] Removing any of the hypotheses of Lemma P–Theorem S breaks either the conclusion or a specific step of the formal proof.

1. *Deleting common integrable domination (`bound_integrable` / `h_bound`), even when every $F_n$ is integrable with bounded $L^1$ norm.*
   - *Zero-end concentration:* On $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$, let $F_n(x) = n\,1_{(0, 1/n]}(x)$ ($n \ge 1$). Every $F_n$ is integrable with $\int_0^\infty F_n = 1$, and for *every* $x > 0$ we have $F_n(x) = 0$ as soon as $n > 1/x$, so $F_n \to 0$ everywhere. Yet $\int_0^\infty F_n = 1 \not\to 0 = \int_0^\infty 0$. In Lemma Q(1), the minimal common dominator is $U_1(x) = \sup_{n \ge 1} F_n(x) = \lfloor 1/x\rfloor$, which takes the value $k$ on $\bigl(\frac{1}{k+1}, \frac{1}{k}\bigr]$ of length $\frac{1}{k(k+1)}$, so
     $$\int_0^\infty U_1(x)\,dx = \sum_{k=1}^\infty k\left(\frac{1}{k} - \frac{1}{k+1}\right) = \sum_{k=1}^\infty \frac{1}{k+1} = +\infty.$$
     Thus `limsup_lintegral_le` cannot invoke `lintegral_iInf`: subtracting $U_n$ from $U_1$ inside `lintegral_iInf_ae` encounters $\infty - \infty$, where `ENNReal.sub_right_inj` fails.
   - *Signed wave-packet with $F_n^+ \neq 0$, $F_n^- \neq 0$, and uniformly bounded $L^1$ norms:* Set $F_n(x) = 2\,1_{(n, n+1]}(x) - 1_{(n+1, n+2]}(x)$ on $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$. Every $F_n$ has $\int F_n^+ = 2 \neq 0$, $\int F_n^- = 1 \neq 0$, $\lVert F_n\rVert_{L^1} = 3$ (uniformly bounded in $n$), and $F_n(x) \to 0$ at every $x > 0$. Yet $\int_0^\infty F_n = 2 - 1 = 1 \not\to 0 = \int_0^\infty 0$: uniform boundedness of the individual norms $\sup_n \int \lvert F_n\rvert \le 3$ cannot substitute for integrability of the *pointwise* supremum $\int \sup_n \lvert F_n\rvert \ge \int_1^\infty 1\,dx = \infty$.

2. *Deleting or swapping the common `∀ᵐ` quantifier in `h_lim`.* In Theorem S, `h_lim : ∀ᵐ a ∂μ, Tendsto (fun n => F n a) l (𝓝 (f a))` requires a *single* full-measure set on which $F_n(a) \to f(a)$ along the entire filter $l$. Three examples on $([0, 1], \mathrm{vol})$, all dominated by $\mathrm{bound}(x) = 1 \in L^1([0, 1])$, separate this quantifier from weaker substitutes:
   - *Point-dependent subsequences (`∀ᵐ x, ∃ (n_k), F_{n_k}(x) → f(x)`):* Let $D_n(x) \in \{0, 1\}$ be the indicator that the $n$-th binary digit of $x \in [0, 1]$ is $1$ ($2^{n-1}$ dyadic intervals of length $2^{-n}$), and take $f \equiv 0$. Off the countable set of dyadic rationals whose binary expansion is eventually $1$, every $x \in [0, 1]$ has infinitely many zero digits, so $\liminf_{n \to \infty} D_n(x) = 0 = f(x)$ and each such $x$ admits an $x$-dependent subsequence $n_k(x)$ with $D_{n_k(x)}(x) = 0 \to 0$. Because there is no *common* full-measure convergence along `atTop`, $\int_0^1 D_n(x)\,dx = 2^{n-1}\cdot 2^{-n} = \frac{1}{2} \not\to 0 = \int_0^1 f$.
   - *Swapping `∀ᶠ t in l` outside `∀ᵐ x` along a first-countable filter:* Along the countably generated right-neighbourhood filter $l = \mathcal N_{>0}(0)$, set $F_t(x) := 1_{(t\mathbb Q) \cap [0, 1]}(x)$ for $t > 0$. For *every* fixed $t > 0$, $(t\mathbb Q) \cap [0, 1]$ is countable and hence null, so the swapped statement $\forall t > 0,\ \forall^{\mathrm{ae}} x \in [0, 1],\ F_t(x) = 0$ holds (this is the quantifier order that `h_bound` uses!). Yet for *every* $x \in (0, 1]$, the sequence $t_m = x/m \to 0^+$ ($m \in \mathbb N_{\ge 1}$) satisfies $x = m t_m \in t_m\mathbb Q$ and hence $F_{t_m}(x) = 1$, while irrational multiples give $0$; thus $\limsup_{t \to 0^+} F_t(x) = 1 > 0 = \liminf_{t \to 0^+} F_t(x)$ at *every* point of $(0, 1]$, and `h_lim` fails on a set of full measure $1$.
   - *Convergence in $L^1$ without `h_lim` disables the tail-supremum step:* For the dyadic typewriter sequence $T_n = 1_{[j 2^{-k},\,(j+1)2^{-k}]}$ ($n = 2^k + j$, $0 \le j < 2^k$), $\int_0^1 T_n = 2^{-k} \to 0$ does converge to $\int_0^1 0 = 0$, yet $\limsup_{n \to \infty} T_n(x) = 1$ at every $x \in [0, 1]$. Inside Lemma Q(1), the tail supremum is $U_n(x) = \sup_{i \ge n} T_i(x) = 1_{[0, 1]}(x)$ for all $n \ge 1$, so `limsup_lintegral_le` yields only $0 \le 1$: `tendsto_setToFun_of_dominated_convergence` genuinely needs `h_lim` to make $\limsup_n \lVert F_n - f\rVert = 0$ a.e. (Convergence in $L^1$ itself is handled separately by `tendsto_setToFun_of_L1`, `SetToL1.lean:906`.)

3. *Deleting `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]` in Theorem S(2) and Lemma P(1).* Let $\iota$ be the set of finite subsets $S \subset [0, 1]$ ordered by inclusion, with upward-directed filter $l = \mathrm{atTop}$ on $\iota$. Set $F_S(x) := 1_S(x)$, $f(x) := 1$, and $\mathrm{bound}(x) := 1$ on $([0, 1], \mathrm{vol})$. Every hypothesis of Theorem S(2) *except* `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]` holds in its strongest form: each $F_S$ is Borel strongly measurable; $\lvert F_S(x)\rvert \le 1 \in L^1([0, 1])$ everywhere (indeed $\lvert F_S(x)\rvert = 0$ $\mathrm{vol}$-a.e. for each $S$, since $S$ is finite!); and at *every* point $x \in [0, 1]$ (empty exceptional set), $F_S(x) = 1 = f(x)$ for all $S \supseteq \{x\}$. Nevertheless, $\int_0^1 F_S(x)\,dx = \mathrm{vol}(S) = 0$ for every $S \in \iota$, whereas $\int_0^1 f(x)\,dx = 1$, so $0 \not\to 1$. Worse still, if $V \subset [0, 1]$ is a Vitali set (§2.3.2.6, Remark item 4) and we put $\widetilde F_S := 1_{S \cap V}$, then each $\widetilde F_S$ is still strongly measurable with $\lvert\widetilde F_S\rvert \le 1$ and $\widetilde F_S(x) \to 1_V(x)$ at *every* $x \in [0, 1]$, yet the limit $1_V$ is not `AEStronglyMeasurable`: without `[l.IsCountablyGenerated]`, Lemma P(1) fails as well because an uncountable union of finite sets need not be measurable or separable-controlled along a single sequence.

4. *The incomplete-codomain convention in Lemma R(1).* When `¬CompleteSpace F`, `setToFun` returns $0 \in F$ on all functions (`SetToL1.lean:630, 1258`), so Lemma R(1) holds trivially as $0 \to 0$ without constructing `L1.setToL1`. Non-vacuous evaluation of $\int a,\,f(a)\,d\mu$ requires `[CompleteSpace G]`, which both $\mathbb R$ and $\mathbb C$ satisfy.

**Remark (Lebesgue's 1910 proof, Bochner's 1933 majorant-and-Egorov proof, Rudin's Fatou argument, and Mathlib's design).** [Expository] In Henri Lebesgue's memoir "Sur l'intégration des fonctions discontinues", *Annales scientifiques de l'École Normale Supérieure* (3) 27 (1910), 361–450, §15 (pp. 375–376) states the theorem for summable real sequences $\lvert f_n\rvert \le F$ on a finite-measure set $E$: he defines the increasing uniform-tail sets $E_n = \{P \in E \mid \forall p > 0,\ \lvert f_{n+p}(P) - f(P)\rvert < \varepsilon\}$, shows $m(E \setminus E_n) \to 0$ by countable additivity, splits $\int_E \lvert f - f_n\rvert \le \varepsilon\,m(E_n) + 2\int_{E \setminus E_n} F$, and invokes his §14 lemma (pp. 374–375) that $\int_e F \to 0$ as $m(e) \to 0$, introducing the phrase *"presque partout"* on p. 376 for null exceptional sets. When $m(E) = \infty$ (as on $(0, \infty)$ for $\Gamma$), the term $\varepsilon\,m(E_n)$ cannot be used without first excising a large-measure tail where $\int F$ is small (`exists_setLIntegral_compl_lt`, `Lebesgue/Sub.lean:158`). Salomon Bochner, "Integration von Funktionen, deren Werte die Elemente eines Vektorraumes sind", *Fundamenta Mathematicae* 20 (1933), 262–276 (§§2–3, pp. 263–267), defined measurability on Euclidean domains as a.e. limits of finite-valued functions in a complete normed space, proved integral convergence under a summable real majorant by Egorov uniform subsets inside a finite cube $W$ plus small-measure and exterior-tail estimates (§3.III *Hilfssatz*, p. 266), and deduced representative-sequence independence by interleaving two sequences (§3.IV, p. 267; [§2.3.2.7.1](#banach-dense-extension), Remark 3). Modern expositions factor that argument into continuous linear extension on $L^1$ plus scalar Lebesgue dominated convergence of $\lVert F_n - f\rVert$ via $\lVert\int g\rVert \le \int \lVert g\rVert$. For the scalar step, Rudin's *Real and Complex Analysis* (3rd ed., Theorem 1.34, pp. 26–27) applies lower Fatou's lemma to the nonnegative real differences $2\,\mathrm{bound} - \lvert F_n - f\rvert$ and cancels $2\int \mathrm{bound} < \infty$. Mathlib separates the two halves cleanly: `SetToL1.lean` executes this modern $L^1$-extension reduction once and for all for every dominated finitely-additive operator $T$ (handling the Bochner integral, conditional expectation `condExp`, and vector-valued set functions uniformly through `L1.setToL1`), while `HasFiniteIntegral.lean` and `Lebesgue/DominatedConvergence.lean` execute the scalar step in $[0, \infty]$ by applying `lintegral_iInf` to $U_n = \sup_{i \ge n} \mathrm{ofReal}\,\lVert F_i - f\rVert$, which places the finite-integral subtraction inside `Lebesgue/Sub.lean` and requires neither $\sigma$-finiteness nor $m(E) < \infty$.

**Use in the archive.** Beyond the Mathlib Gamma and Mellin consumers of Theorem S(3) and Corollary T, archive A2 (`lean.tar.gz` SHA-256 `9631b4f020199e580ba86178cb56c6e9b09f690c2369a80dc4334aa93f49c7fc`) invokes the declarations read in this subsection across `lean/src/` at 65 occurrences of `tendsto_integral_of_dominated_convergence`, 71 of `tendsto_integral_filter_of_dominated_convergence`, 9 of `tendsto_lintegral_of_dominated_convergence`, 143 of `hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_deriv_le`, and 12 of `differentiableAt_Gamma` (in addition to `GIntegral.l21a` via `intervalIntegral_tendsto_integral_Ioi`, read in §2.3.2.6). Two representative calls illustrate why the countably-generated-filter signature of Theorem S(2) is needed directly rather than only through $\mathbb N$-sequences:

- In `src/Formalism/HermitianCurvature/HermitianCurvatureProofs/WeightedL2ConstMul.lean:302–355`, lemmas `L1` (line 310) and `L2` (line 340) pass to the continuous smoothing limit $\varepsilon \to 0^+$ along `l = 𝓝[>] (0 : ℝ)` on an abstract measure space $(B.\Omega, B.\mu)$: `L1` dominates $\theta_{\varepsilon, \sigma}(B.G(\omega))\,f(\omega)$ by $\lVert f(\omega)\rVert$ via $0 \le \theta_{\varepsilon, \sigma} \le 1$ (`ThetaRange`) to recover $\int f\,dB.\mu$, while `L2` dominates complex-valued logarithmic derivatives $(\operatorname{ld}_p \theta_{\varepsilon, \sigma})(B.G(\omega))\,h(\omega)$ ($p \ge 1$) by $e\cdot p!\,(4\sigma)^p \lVert h(\omega)\rVert$ for $\varepsilon > 0$ (`self_mem_nhdsWithin`) to force the limit $0$.
- In `src/GroundTheory/HeightsAndTheta/Heights/OrbitLimits/PLelongWeightDiff.lean:30–75` (line 51), the regularised Lelong-weight difference `chartDens J A (D ε) (vecCons g φ)` is passed to $\varepsilon \to 0^+$ along `l = 𝓝[>] (0 : ℝ)`: here `h_meas` and `h_bound` hold only *eventually* on $\{ \varepsilon > 0 \} \in \mathcal N_{>0}(0)$ (lines 56–69), while `h_lim` holds off the algebraic zero locus $\{y \mid P(y) = 0\}$ of measure zero (`CutOffDominated.ae_eval_ne_zero`, lines 70–75) — matching the exact quantifier split of Theorem S(2).

Whether these individual call sites lie in the compiled dependency closure of `src/Main.lean` is not re-derived here.

Sections 2.3.2.7.4–6 follow a product from scalar Young's inequality to an integral of a finite weighted family and then to a Bochner pairing. The background reading for §§2.3.2.7.1–3 is the dense linear extension used in the Bochner integral, Riesz–Fischer completeness, and the integral Minkowski estimate. For the product results, the present §2.3.2.7 gives the $L^1$ extension and §2.3.2.7.6 states the required measurability and integrability conditions directly. Minkowski and Riesz–Fischer are applications of the estimates in the order explained below. [Predecessor source pointers](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-2-hölder-and-bochner-products)

<a id="banach-dense-extension"></a>

##### 2.3.2.7.1 Continuous linear extension from a dense subspace and the Bochner $L^1$ integral

**Motivation.** In [§2.3.2.7](#2327-dominated-convergence-through-the-l1-extension-the-filter-to-sequence-reduction-the-decreasing-tail-supremum-step-and-local-uniform-domination-of-the-complex-gamma-kernel), step 3 of the Bochner dominated convergence proof reduced vector-valued integral convergence to scalar $L^1$-norm convergence by rewriting `setToFun` on a complete target space $F$ as a continuous linear operator `L1.setToL1 hT : (α →₁[μ] E) →L[ℝ] F` of operator norm at most $\max(C, 0)$ ($1$ for the Bochner integral), while retaining `ContinuousLinearMap.extend` and simple-function $L^1$ density as named `[Lean]` inputs. Chapter 14 ([§14.4.2.3.1](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#144231-finite-measure-simple-functions-in-lp), building on the two-case induction in [§14.4.2.3](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#14423-continuous-functions-are-dense-in-l2-of-the-circle)) has already proved `Lp.simpleFunc.denseRange`: for any measure space $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$, any normed additive commutative group $E$, and $1 \le p < \infty$, the integrable simple classes form a dense subgroup of $L^p(\mu; E)$, without completeness or separability of $E$ and without $\sigma$-finiteness of $\mu$. We cite that density theorem directly and do not repeat the nearest-point sequence or finite-fiber argument.

What remains to open is the functional-analytic bridge itself: how a bounded linear operator defined only on integrable simple classes — where $\int s\,d\mu = \sum_{x \in s(\alpha)} \mu.\mathrm{real}(s^{-1}\{x\}) \cdot x$ is a finite sum in $E$ — becomes a genuine continuous linear operator on all of $L^1(\mu; E)$, why completeness is required of the **target space** $F$ rather than of the ambient domain $L^1(\mu; E)$, why the limit is independent of the chosen sequence of simple approximants and of null-set modifications, and how the constants enter. In the pinned Mathlib commit `0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474`, `ContinuousLinearMap.extend` does not live in `Topology/Algebra/Module/Basic.lean` (which houses submodule topological closures and pointwise limits of globally defined linear maps); its actual production proof sits in `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean:58–136`, resting on the uniform-space Cauchy extension engine in `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean:431–506` and the dense-inducing equalizer machinery in `Mathlib/Topology/DenseEmbedding.lean:126–190, 337–384`. Reading those proof bodies alongside `SetToL1.lean:88–656, 737–938`, `Bochner/L1.lean:77–636`, and `Bochner/Basic.lean:156–320, 904–971` separates three kinds of constants:

1. The **embedding distortion** $N$ bounds the inverse on the dense image: $\lVert x\rVert_E \le N\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}$. It gives $\lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert\le N\lVert f\rVert$. The simple-class inclusion is isometric, so $N=1$.
2. The **set-operator domination** $C$ comes from $\lVert T(s)\rVert\le C\mu.\mathrm{real}(s)$ on finite-measure measurable sets. Its operator-norm bound is $\max(C,0)$. For the Bochner integral, $\mathrm{weightedSMul}\,\mu$ gives $C=1$.
3. The **total mass** $\mu(X)$ enters when an essential bound $\lVert f(x)\rVert\le M$ is converted into the finite-measure estimate $\lVert f\rVert_{L^1}\le M\mu(X)$. The contraction on $L^1$ uses the preceding two bounds.

---

**Examples (two complete hand calculations on finite measure spaces).** Before stating the formal lemmas, we trace two calculations through simple-function evaluation, $L^1$ Cauchy bounds, independence of the approximating sequence, and operator-norm constants: first, a complex-valued function on a finite measure space of total mass $2 \neq 1$; second, a genuinely infinite-dimensional Banach-valued function into $\ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ on $([0, 1], \mathrm{vol})$, paired with a dominated operator of constant $C = \tfrac52 \neq 1$.

1. *Hand calculation 1: complex-valued integration on $([0, 2], \mathrm{vol})$ ($\mu([0, 2]) = 2$), representative independence, $\mathbb C$-linearity, and separating $N$, $C$, and $\mu(\alpha)$.* [Expository] Equip $X = [0, 2]$ with Borel Lebesgue measure $\mu = \mathrm{vol}|_{[0, 2]}$ of total mass $\mu(X) = 2 < \infty$, take target $E = F = \mathbb C$ (`[CompleteSpace ℂ]`), and define
   $$f(x) := 3x\,1_{[0, 1)}(x) + 4i\,x^2\,1_{[1, 2]}(x) \qquad (x \in [0, 2]).$$
   For each integer $n \ge 1$, split $[0, 1)$ into $n$ intervals $J_{n,k} = \bigl[\frac{k-1}{n}, \frac{k}{n}\bigr)$ and $[1, 2]$ into $n$ intervals $K_{n,k} = \bigl[1 + \frac{k-1}{n}, 1 + \frac{k}{n}\bigr)$ ($1 \le k \le n$, with the endpoint $2$ included in $K_{n,n}$), each of measure $\mu(J_{n,k}) = \mu(K_{n,k}) = \frac{1}{n}$. Compare two distinct sequences in $\mathcal L^1_s([0, 2]; \mathbb C)$: the left-endpoint step functions
   $$s_n(x) := \sum_{k=1}^n \frac{3(k-1)}{n}\,1_{J_{n,k}}(x) + \sum_{k=1}^n 4i\left(1 + \frac{k-1}{n}\right)^2 1_{K_{n,k}}(x),$$
   and the right-endpoint step functions perturbed by an unbounded complex spike on the three-point null set $\{0, 1, 2\}$,
   $$r_n(x) := \sum_{k=1}^n \frac{3k}{n}\,1_{J_{n,k}}(x) + \sum_{k=1}^n 4i\left(1 + \frac{k}{n}\right)^2 1_{K_{n,k}}(x) + (5 - 7i)\,n\,1_{\{0, 1, 2\}}(x).$$

   - *Simple-function integrals and null-set invariance.* Evaluating `SimpleFunc.integral` (`Bochner/L1.lean:194–203`) on $s_n$ sums $\mu.\mathrm{real}(J_{n,k}) = \frac{1}{n}$ and $\mu.\mathrm{real}(K_{n,k}) = \frac{1}{n}$:
     $$I_0([s_n]) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^n \frac{3(k-1)}{n} + \frac{4i}{n}\sum_{k=1}^n \left(1 + \frac{2(k-1)}{n} + \frac{(k-1)^2}{n^2}\right) = \frac{3(n-1)}{2n} + i\,\frac{2(14n^2 - 9n + 1)}{3n^2}.$$
     On $r_n$, the three-point set $\{0, 1, 2\}$ has $\mu(\{0, 1, 2\}) = 0$, so `weightedSMul_null` (`Bochner/L1.lean:108`) and `setToSimpleFunc_congr` (`FinMeasAdditive.lean:390–400`) annihilate the $(5 - 7i)n$ spike regardless of its amplitude:
     $$I_0([r_n]) = \frac{3(n+1)}{2n} + i\,\frac{2(14n^2 + 9n + 1)}{3n^2}.$$

   - *Exact rational $L^1$ errors, dyadic Cauchy budget, and representative-sequence independence.* Because $f - s_n$ is purely real and nonnegative on $[0, 1)$ and purely imaginary with nonnegative imaginary part on $[1, 2]$, its complex modulus equals $\Re(f - s_n)$ on $[0, 1)$ and $\Im(f - s_n)$ on $[1, 2]$. Integrating piecewise gives exact rational $L^1$ norms without irrational square roots:
     $$\lVert [f] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1} = \left(\frac{3}{2} - \frac{3(n-1)}{2n}\right) + \left(\frac{28}{3} - \frac{28n^2 - 18n + 2}{3n^2}\right) = \frac{3}{2n} + \frac{18n - 2}{3n^2} = \frac{45n - 4}{6n^2},$$
     $$\lVert [r_n] - [f]\rVert_{L^1} = \left(\frac{3(n+1)}{2n} - \frac{3}{2}\right) + \left(\frac{28n^2 + 18n + 2}{3n^2} - \frac{28}{3}\right) = \frac{3}{2n} + \frac{18n + 2}{3n^2} = \frac{45n + 4}{6n^2}.$$
     Off $\{0, 1, 2\}$, $r_n - s_n$ equals $\frac{3}{n}$ on $[0, 1)$ and $4i\bigl((1 + \frac{k}{n})^2 - (1 + \frac{k-1}{n})^2\bigr)$ on $K_{n,k}$, which has imaginary coefficients that telescope over $k = 1, \dots, n$ to $4(2^2 - 1^2) = 12$:
     $$\lVert [r_n] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{3}{n} + \frac{12}{n} = \frac{15}{n} = \lVert [f] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1} + \lVert [r_n] - [f]\rVert_{L^1} \to 0.$$
     Along dyadic refinements $n = 2^a \mid m = 2^b$ ($a \le b$), $s_m \ge s_n$ componentwise on both halves, so $\lVert [s_m] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{45n - 4}{6n^2} - \frac{45m - 4}{6m^2}$, and the contraction $\lVert I_0\rVert \le 1$ forces $\lvert I_0([s_m]) - I_0([s_n])\rvert \le \lVert [s_m] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1}$, making $(I_0([s_{2^a}]))$ Cauchy in the complete target $\mathbb C$. Moreover, representative independence is quantitative:
     $$\bigl\lvert I_0([r_n]) - I_0([s_n])\bigr\rvert = \left\lvert \frac{3}{n} + \frac{12}{n}i\right\rvert = \frac{3\sqrt{17}}{n} < \frac{15}{n} = 1 \cdot \lVert [r_n] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1},$$
     and both sequences converge in $\mathbb C$ to the same extended integral:
     $$\int_0^2 f(x)\,dx = \lim_{n \to \infty} I_0([s_n]) = \lim_{n \to \infty} I_0([r_n]) = \frac{3}{2} + \frac{28}{3}i.$$

     | $n$ | $I_0([s_n])$ | $I_0([r_n])$ | $\lVert [f] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [r_n] - [f]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [r_n] - [s_n]\rVert_{L^1}$ |
     |---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
     | $1$ | $0 + 4i$ | $3 + 16i$ | $\tfrac{41}{6}$ | $\tfrac{49}{6}$ | $15$ |
     | $2$ | $\tfrac{3}{4} + \tfrac{13}{2}i$ | $\tfrac{9}{4} + \tfrac{25}{2}i$ | $\tfrac{43}{12}$ | $\tfrac{47}{12}$ | $\tfrac{15}{2}$ |
     | $4$ | $\tfrac{9}{8} + \tfrac{63}{8}i$ | $\tfrac{15}{8} + \tfrac{87}{8}i$ | $\tfrac{11}{6}$ | $\tfrac{23}{12}$ | $\tfrac{15}{4}$ |
     | $8$ | $\tfrac{21}{16} + \tfrac{275}{32}i$ | $\tfrac{27}{16} + \tfrac{323}{32}i$ | $\tfrac{89}{96}$ | $\tfrac{91}{96}$ | $\tfrac{15}{8}$ |
     | $16$ | $\tfrac{45}{32} + \tfrac{1147}{128}i$ | $\tfrac{51}{32} + \tfrac{1243}{128}i$ | $\tfrac{179}{384}$ | $\tfrac{181}{384}$ | $\tfrac{15}{16}$ |
     | $64$ | $\tfrac{189}{128} + \tfrac{18923}{2048}i$ | $\tfrac{195}{128} + \tfrac{19307}{2048}i$ | $\tfrac{719}{6144}$ | $\tfrac{721}{6144}$ | $\tfrac{15}{64}$ |

   - *$\mathbb C$-scalar homogeneity and the three kinds of norm constants on one example.* Multiplying by $c = 2 - 3i \in \mathbb C$ via `integral_smul` (`Bochner/Basic.lean:275`, backed by `L1.integral_eq' ℂ` at `Bochner/L1.lean:560`) gives
     $$\int_0^2 (2 - 3i)f(x)\,dx = (2 - 3i)\left(\frac{3}{2} + \frac{28}{3}i\right) = 31 + \frac{85}{6}i.$$
     Comparing norm bounds on $f$ illustrates all three kinds of constants:
     - *Signed modulus vs. $L^1$ norm ($C = 1$, $N = 1$):* $\bigl\lvert\int_0^2 f\,dx\bigr\rvert = \frac{\sqrt{3217}}{6} \approx 9.4531 < \frac{65}{6} \approx 10.8333 = \lVert [f]\rVert_{L^1}$ because the real lobe on $[0, 1)$ and the imaginary lobe on $[1, 2]$ are orthogonal in $\mathbb R^2$; on the constant-phase integrand $\lvert f\rvert$, equality $\int_0^2 \lvert f\rvert\,dx = \frac{65}{6} = 1 \cdot \lVert [\lvert f\rvert]\rVert_{L^1}$ is attained, so $\lVert \mathrm{L1.integralCLM}\rVert = 1$ despite $\mu([0, 2]) = 2$.
     - *Finite-measure $L^\infty$ bound ($\mu([0, 2]) = 2$):* since $\sup_{x \in [0, 2]} \lvert f(x)\rvert = 16$, `norm_integral_le_of_norm_le_const` gives $16 \cdot \mu([0, 2]) = 32$: the factor $\mu(X) = 2$ comes from bounding $\lVert [f]\rVert_{L^1} \le \mu(X)\,\lVert f\rVert_\infty$, not from `L1.setToL1`.
     - *Non-isometric dense embedding ($N = 3$):* if we replace `coeToLp` by the scaled dense embedding $e_{(3)} : L^1_s([0, 2]; \mathbb C) \to_L L^1([0, 2]; \mathbb C)$, $e_{(3)}(s) := \frac{1}{3}\,\mathrm{coeToLp}(s)$, then $\lVert s\rVert_{L^1_s} = 3\,\lVert e_{(3)}(s)\rVert_{L^1}$ ($N = 3$ in `opNorm_extend_le`), and $(I_0.\mathrm{extend}\,e_{(3)})(y) = 3\,\mathrm{L1.integral}(y)$ attains $\bigl\lvert(I_0.\mathrm{extend}\,e_{(3)})([\lvert f\rvert])\bigr\rvert = \frac{65}{2} = 3 \cdot \frac{65}{6}$, showing that the factor $N$ in Theorem 2.3.2.7c is sharp.

2. *Hand calculation 2: infinite-dimensional Banach-valued integration into $\ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ on $([0, 1], \mathrm{vol})$ and a dominated operator with $C = \tfrac52$.* [Expository] Equip $[0, 1]$ with Borel Lebesgue measure $\mu = \mathrm{vol}|_{[0, 1]}$ ($\mu([0, 1]) = 1$), and let $E = \ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ be the Banach space of absolutely summable real sequences $u = (u_m)_{m \ge 1}$ with $\lVert u\rVert_{\ell^1} = \sum_{m=1}^\infty \lvert u_m\rvert$ (the same infinite-dimensional test space used in Bochner's 1933 §7, p. 275, eq. (11)). Partition $(0, 1]$ into the dyadic intervals $D_k = (2^{-k}, 2^{-k+1}]$ ($k \ge 1$, $\mu(D_k) = 2^{-k}$). For each $k \ge 1$, define the tail vector $g_k \in \ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ by $(g_k)_m := 3^{-m}$ for $m \ge k$ and $0$ for $1 \le m < k$, and set
   $$g(x) := \sum_{k=1}^\infty g_k\,1_{D_k}(x) \qquad (x \in (0, 1]), \qquad g(0) := 0.$$
   At every $x \in D_k$, $g(x) = g_k$ has infinitely many nonzero coordinates and exact norm $\lVert g_k\rVert_{\ell^1} = \sum_{m=k}^\infty 3^{-m} = \frac{3}{2}\,3^{-k}$. Compare two simple-function approximations in $\mathcal L^1_s([0, 1]; \ell^1)$:
   $$s_N(x) := \sum_{k=1}^N g_k\,1_{D_k}(x), \qquad t_N(x) := \sum_{k=1}^N \left(\sum_{m=k}^N 3^{-m} e_m\right) 1_{D_k}(x),$$
   where $s_N$ truncates only the domain dyadic index $k \le N$, whereas $t_N$ simultaneously truncates both the domain $k \le N$ and the Banach coordinate tail $m \le N$.

   - *Exact $L^1([0, 1]; \ell^1)$ norms and representative-sequence comparison.* Summing over the disjoint intervals $D_k$ gives
     $$\lVert [g]\rVert_{L^1([0,1]; \ell^1)} = \sum_{k=1}^\infty 2^{-k}\cdot \frac{3}{2}\,3^{-k} = \frac{3}{2}\sum_{k=1}^\infty 6^{-k} = \frac{3}{10}, \qquad \lVert [g] - [s_N]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{3}{2}\sum_{k=N+1}^\infty 6^{-k} = \frac{3}{10}\,6^{-N}.$$
     On each $D_k$ ($1 \le k \le N$), $s_N(x) - t_N(x) = \sum_{m=N+1}^\infty 3^{-m} e_m$ has $\ell^1$ norm $\frac{1}{2}\,3^{-N}$ independent of $k$, while both vanish on $[0, 2^{-N}]$. Hence
     $$\lVert [s_N] - [t_N]\rVert_{L^1} = \left(\sum_{k=1}^N 2^{-k}\right)\frac{1}{2}\,3^{-N} = \frac{2^N - 1}{2\cdot 6^N} \to 0, \qquad \lVert [g] - [t_N]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{2^N - 1}{2\cdot 6^N} + \frac{3}{10}\,6^{-N} = \frac{5\cdot 2^N - 2}{10\cdot 6^N} \to 0.$$

   - *Bochner integral in $\ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$.* Applying `SimpleFunc.integral` gives $(I_0([s_N]))_m = 3^{-m}\sum_{k=1}^{\min(m, N)} 2^{-k} = 3^{-m}(1 - 2^{-\min(m, N)})$ for all $m \ge 1$, whereas $(I_0([t_N]))_m = 3^{-m}(1 - 2^{-m}) = 3^{-m} - 6^{-m}$ for $1 \le m \le N$ and $0$ for $m > N$. In the complete space $\ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$, both converge in $\ell^1$-norm to the vector $v = \int_0^1 g(x)\,dx \in \ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ with coordinates
     $$v_m = 3^{-m} - 6^{-m} \qquad (m \ge 1; \text{ first four coordinates } \tfrac{1}{6},\ \tfrac{1}{12},\ \tfrac{7}{216},\ \tfrac{5}{432}),$$
     satisfying $\lVert v - I_0([s_N])\rVert_{\ell^1} = \sum_{m=N+1}^\infty 3^{-m}(2^{-N} - 2^{-m}) = \frac{3}{10}\,6^{-N} = \lVert [g] - [s_N]\rVert_{L^1}$ and $\lVert v - I_0([t_N])\rVert_{\ell^1} = \sum_{m=N+1}^\infty (3^{-m} - 6^{-m}) = \frac{5\cdot 2^N - 2}{10\cdot 6^N} = \lVert [g] - [t_N]\rVert_{L^1}$. Because all coordinates are nonnegative, $\lVert v\rVert_{\ell^1} = \sum_{m=1}^\infty (3^{-m} - 6^{-m}) = \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{5} = \frac{3}{10} = \lVert [g]\rVert_{L^1}$ attains the $C = 1$ operator norm in infinite dimensions.

   - *`L1.setToL1` with a non-unit constant $C = \tfrac52$.* Let $A : \ell^1(\mathbb N_{\ge 1}) \to_L \mathbb R$ be the bounded functional $A(u) := \frac{5}{2}\sum_{m=1}^\infty (-1)^{m-1} u_m$ ($\lVert A\rVert = \frac{5}{2}$), and set $T(s) := \mu.\mathrm{real}(s)\cdot A \in \ell^1 \to_L \mathbb R$. Then `DominatedFinMeasAdditive μ T (5/2)` holds with $C = \frac{5}{2}$ (sharp on $e_1\,1_{[0, 1]}$, where `setToL1 hT` evaluates $\frac{5}{2} = \frac{5}{2}\cdot 1$). On $[g]$, both simple sequences converge under `setToL1SCLM` to
     $$(\mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT)([g]) = A(v) = \frac{5}{2}\sum_{m=1}^\infty (-1)^{m-1}(3^{-m} - 6^{-m}) = \frac{5}{2}\left(\frac{1/3}{1 + 1/3} - \frac{1/6}{1 + 1/6}\right) = \frac{5}{2}\left(\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{7}\right) = \frac{15}{56},$$
     strictly below $C\,\lVert [g]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{5}{2}\cdot\frac{3}{10} = \frac{3}{4} = \frac{42}{56}$ by alternating-sign cancellation:

     | $N$ | $\lVert [g] - [s_N]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [g] - [t_N]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [s_N] - [t_N]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $(\mathrm{setToL1}\,hT)([s_N])$ | $(\mathrm{setToL1}\,hT)([t_N])$ |
     |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
     | $1$ | $\tfrac{1}{20}$ | $\tfrac{2}{15}$ | $\tfrac{1}{12}$ | $\tfrac{5}{16}$ | $\tfrac{5}{12}$ |
     | $2$ | $\tfrac{1}{120}$ | $\tfrac{1}{20}$ | $\tfrac{1}{24}$ | $\tfrac{25}{96}$ | $\tfrac{5}{24}$ |
     | $3$ | $\tfrac{1}{720}$ | $\tfrac{19}{1080}$ | $\tfrac{7}{432}$ | $\tfrac{155}{576}$ | $\tfrac{125}{432}$ |
     | $4$ | $\tfrac{1}{4320}$ | $\tfrac{13}{2160}$ | $\tfrac{5}{864}$ | $\tfrac{925}{3456}$ | $\tfrac{25}{96}$ |
     | $8$ | $\tfrac{1}{5598720}$ | $\tfrac{71}{933120}$ | $\tfrac{85}{1119744}$ | $\tfrac{1199725}{4478976}$ | $\tfrac{299825}{1119744}$ |

---

**Definitions (uniform inducing maps, dense-inducing limits, and the four-step $L^1$ construction).**

1. *Uniform inducing and dense inducing maps (`DenseEmbedding.lean:38–55, 140–141`, `UniformEmbedding.lean:34–44, 110–114`).* Let $(\beta, \mathcal U_\beta)$ and $(\alpha, \mathcal U_\alpha)$ be uniform spaces. A map $e : \beta \to \alpha$ satisfies `IsUniformInducing e` if the uniformity $\mathcal U_\beta$ equals the pullback $\mathrm{comap}\,(e \times e)\,\mathcal U_\alpha$; equivalently (`isUniformInducing_iff'`), $e$ is uniformly continuous and $\mathrm{comap}\,(e \times e)\,\mathcal U_\alpha \le \mathcal U_\beta$. In normed spaces $E, E_\ell$, for an additive homomorphism $e : E \to E_\ell$, this pullback condition means that smallness of $\lVert e(x) - e(x')\rVert_{E_\ell} = \lVert e(x - x')\rVert_{E_\ell}$ controls $\lVert x - x'\rVert_E$. Combining `IsUniformInducing e` with `DenseRange e` ($\overline{e(\beta)} = \alpha$) produces `di : IsDenseInducing e`, so $\mathcal N(b) = \mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(e(b)))$ for every $b \in \beta$ (`nhds_eq_comap`) and $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))$ is a proper (`NeBot`) filter on $\beta$ for every $a \in \alpha$ (`comap_nhds_neBot`). Given a topological space $\gamma$ and $f : \beta \to \gamma$, `IsDenseInducing.extend di f : α → γ` sets
   $$\psi(a) := \operatorname{limUnder}\,\bigl(\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))\bigr)\,f = \varepsilon\bigl(c \in \gamma \mid \mathrm{map}\,f\,(\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))) \le \mathcal N(c)\bigr)$$
   using Hilbert's choice operator `Classical.epsilon` seeded at `f (di.dense.some a)` (`Defs/Filter.lean:247–254`, `DenseEmbedding.lean:140–141`).
2. *Totalized continuous linear extension (`Extend.lean:58–82`).* For topological modules $E, E_\ell$ over $\mathbb K$ and a Hausdorff complete uniform topological module $F$ over $\mathbb K_2$ (`[IsUniformAddGroup E] [IsUniformAddGroup F] [T0Space F] [CompleteSpace F]`), given $f : E \to_{SL[\sigma_{12}]} F$ and $e : E \to_L E_\ell$, `ContinuousLinearMap.extend f e : Eₗ →SL[σ₁₂] F` evaluates `(h.2.isDenseInducing h.1).extend f` if `h : DenseRange e ∧ IsUniformInducing e` holds, and the zero operator $0$ otherwise.
3. *Simple-class $L^1$ subspace and `L1.setToL1` (`SimpleFuncDenseLp.lean:370–450, 639–685`, `SetToL1.lean:112–118, 282–288, 418–420, 630–634`).* The additive subgroup `Lp.simpleFunc E p μ` (written $\alpha \to_{1s}[\mu] E$ at $p = 1$) consists of classes $f \in \mathrm{Lp}\,E\,p\,\mu$ admitting a simple representative $s : \alpha \to_s E$ with `AEEqFun.mk s _ = f`; it carries the induced $L^p$ norm $\lVert s\rVert_{L^p_s} = \lVert (s : \mathrm{Lp}\,E\,p\,\mu)\rVert_{L^p}$, making the continuous $\mathbb K$-linear inclusion `coeToLp α E 𝕜` an exact isometry. For $hT : \mathrm{DominatedFinMeasAdditive}\,\mu\,T\,C$, `setToL1S T f := (toSimpleFunc f).setToSimpleFunc T` is bundled via `LinearMap.mkContinuous` into `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT : (α →₁ₛ[μ] E) →L[ℝ] F`, extended under `[CompleteSpace F]` to `L1.setToL1 hT := (setToL1SCLM α E μ hT).extend (coeToLp α E ℝ)`, and wrapped at the raw function level by `setToFun μ T hT g` (returning `L1.setToL1 hT (hf.toL1 g)` when `CompleteSpace F` and `hf : Integrable g μ` hold, and $0$ otherwise). [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean:58–136`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean#L58-L136) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean:431–506`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean#L431-L506) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/DenseEmbedding.lean:126–190`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/DenseEmbedding.lean#L126-L190) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean:398–656`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean#L398-L656)
