**Lemmas and theorems.**

**Lemma 2.3.2.7a (dense Cauchy approximation, target completeness, and representative-sequence independence).** Let $(\beta, \mathcal U_\beta)$, $(\alpha, \mathcal U_\alpha)$, and $(\gamma, \mathcal U_\gamma)$ be uniform spaces, let $e : \beta \to \alpha$ satisfy `IsUniformInducing e` and `DenseRange e`, and let $f : \beta \to \gamma$ satisfy `UniformContinuous f`. Set $\psi := (h_e.\mathrm{isDenseInducing}\,h_{\mathrm{dense}}).\mathrm{extend}\,f : \alpha \to \gamma$.

1. *Cauchy filter convergence in a complete target (`uniformly_extend_exists`, `uniformly_extend_spec`).* Assume `[CompleteSpace γ]`. For every $a \in \alpha$, the pulled-back filter $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))$ is a proper Cauchy filter on $\beta$, its image $\mathrm{map}\,f\,(\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a)))$ is a Cauchy filter on $\gamma$, there exists $c \in \gamma$ such that $f \to c$ along $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))$, and $f \to \psi(a)$ along $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))$.
2. *Independence of approximating sequences and agreement on $e(\beta)$ (`Tendsto.limUnder_eq`, `uniformly_extend_of_ind`).* Assume further `[T0Space γ]` (so $\gamma$ is Hausdorff, `[T2Space γ]`). If `[CompleteSpace γ]` holds, then for every $a \in \alpha$ and **every** sequence $(x_n)_{n \in \mathbb N}$ in $\beta$ satisfying $e(x_n) \to a$ in $\alpha$, the sequence $(f(x_n))_{n \in \mathbb N}$ converges in $\gamma$ to $\psi(a)$; in particular, $\lim_{n \to \infty} f(x_n) = \lim_{n \to \infty} f(x'_n) = \psi(a)$ for any two sequences with $e(x_n) \to a$ and $e(x'_n) \to a$. Moreover, even without `[CompleteSpace γ]`, $\psi(e(b)) = f(b)$ holds for every $b \in \beta$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean:440–469,500–501`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean#L440-L469) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean:78–80,153–174,370–373`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L153-L174) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/Separation/Hausdorff.lean:274–299`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Separation/Hausdorff.lean#L274-L299)

**Lemma 2.3.2.7b (uniform continuity, closed-equalizer linearity, and uniqueness of `ContinuousLinearMap.extend`).**

1. *Uniform continuity (`uniformContinuous_uniformly_extend`).* Under the hypotheses of Lemma 2.3.2.7a(1) (`[CompleteSpace γ]`), $\psi : \alpha \to \gamma$ is uniformly continuous (and therefore continuous).
2. *Continuous semilinear extension and uniqueness (`ContinuousLinearMap.extend`, `extend_eq`, `extend_unique`).* Let $E, F$ be uniform additive commutative groups (`[IsUniformAddGroup E] [IsUniformAddGroup F] [T0Space F] [CompleteSpace F]`), let $E_\ell$ be a uniform additive commutative monoid with `[ContinuousAdd Eₗ]`, let $\sigma_{12} : \mathbb K \to \mathbb K_2$ be a ring homomorphism with `[ContinuousConstSMul 𝕜 Eₗ] [ContinuousConstSMul 𝕜₂ F]`, and let $f : E \to_{SL[\sigma_{12}]} F$ and $e : E \to_L E_\ell$ satisfy `DenseRange e` and `IsUniformInducing e`. Then `f.extend e : Eₗ →SL[σ₁₂] F` is a well-defined continuous $\sigma_{12}$-semilinear map satisfying `(f.extend e) (e x) = f x` for all $x \in E$, and if $g : E_\ell \to_{SL[\sigma_{12}]} F$ is any continuous $\sigma_{12}$-semilinear map with $g \circ e = f$, then `f.extend e = g`. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean:471–505`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/UniformEmbedding.lean#L471-L505) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/DenseEmbedding.lean:337–384`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/DenseEmbedding.lean#L337-L384) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean:58–101`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean#L58-L101)

**Theorem 2.3.2.7c (operator-norm control: general inverse-norm constant $N$ versus isometric extension).** Let $\mathbb K, \mathbb K_2$ be nontrivially normed fields with an isometric ring homomorphism $\sigma_{12} : \mathbb K \to \mathbb K_2$ (`[RingHomIsometric σ₁₂]`), let $E, E_\ell, F$ be normed spaces ($E, E_\ell$ over $\mathbb K$, $F$ over $\mathbb K_2$), and assume `[CompleteSpace F]`.

1. *General bounded-below embedding (`isUniformEmbedding_of_bound`, `opNorm_extend_le`).* Let $e : E \to_L E_\ell$ satisfy `DenseRange e` and $\forall x \in E,\ \lVert x\rVert_E \le N\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}$ for some $N \in \mathbb R_{\ge 0}$. Then $e$ is a uniform embedding (hence `IsUniformInducing e`), and for every $f : E \to_{SL[\sigma_{12}]} F$,
   $$\lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert \le N\,\lVert f\rVert.$$
   If $e$ is an isometry ($\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell} = \lVert x\rVert_E$ for all $x \in E$, so $N = 1$), then $\lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert = \lVert f\rVert$.
2. *Factorization without a domain norm (`LinearMap.extendOfNorm`, `opNorm_extendOfNorm_le`).* If $E$ is an unnormed $\mathbb K$-module, $E_\ell$ is a seminormed $\mathbb K$-space, $e : E \to_\ell E_\ell$ has `DenseRange e`, and $f : E \to_{s\ell[\sigma_{12}]} F$ satisfies $\forall x \in E,\ \lVert f(x)\rVert_F \le C\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}$ with $C \ge 0$, then `f.extendOfNorm e := (f.compLeftInverse e).extend (LinearMap.range e).subtypeL` satisfies `(f.extendOfNorm e) (e x) = f x` for all $x \in E$ and $\lVert f.\mathrm{extendOfNorm}\,e\rVert \le C$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean:115–235`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean#L115-L235) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/ContinuousLinearMap.lean:157–160`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/ContinuousLinearMap.lean#L157-L160) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Basic.lean:173–202,477–487`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Operator/Basic.lean#L173-L202)

**Theorem 2.3.2.7d (from simple functions to `L1.setToL1`, `L1.integral`, and `MeasureTheory.integral`).** Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be a measure space, let $E, F$ be normed $\mathbb R$-spaces, and let $T : \mathcal P(\alpha) \to (E \to_L F)$ satisfy $hT : \mathrm{DominatedFinMeasAdditive}\,\mu\,T\,C$ ($C \in \mathbb R$).

1. *Simple-class operator (`setToL1S`, `setToL1SCLM`, `norm_setToL1SCLM_le'`).* For every $f \in \alpha \to_{1s}[\mu] E$, `setToL1S T f` is independent of the chosen integrable simple representative, $\mathbb R$-linear, and bounded by $\lVert \mathrm{setToL1S}\,T\,f\rVert_F \le C\,\lVert f\rVert_{L^1}$, yielding `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT : (α →₁ₛ[μ] E) →L[ℝ] F` with $\lVert \mathrm{setToL1SCLM}\,\alpha\,E\,\mu\,hT\rVert \le \max(C, 0)$ (and $\le C$ when $C \ge 0$).
2. *Dense isometric extension on $L^1$ (`L1.setToL1`, `setToL1_eq_setToL1SCLM`, `setToL1_unique`, `norm_setToL1_le'`, `setToL1_smul`).* Assume `[CompleteSpace F]`. Using `Lp.simpleFunc.denseRange one_ne_top` (proved in Chapter 14, [§14.4.2.3.1](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#144231-finite-measure-simple-functions-in-lp)) and the isometric subgroup inclusion `coeToLp α E ℝ` (`Lp.simpleFunc.isUniformInducing`, $N = 1$), `L1.setToL1 hT : (α →₁[μ] E) →L[ℝ] F` is the unique continuous $\mathbb R$-linear map agreeing with `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT` on $\alpha \to_{1s}[\mu] E$. It satisfies $\lVert \mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\rVert \le \max(C, 0)$ ($\le C$ when $C \ge 0$), evaluates `(L1.setToL1 hT) (indicatorConstLp 1 hs hμs x) = T s x` for measurable $s$ with $\mu(s) \neq \infty$, and satisfies $\mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\,(c \cdot f) = c \cdot \mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\,f$ over any normed ring $\mathbb K$ acting boundedly on $E, F$ whenever $\forall c, s, x,\ T(s)(c \cdot x) = c \cdot T(s)(x)$.
3. *Specialization to the Bochner integral (`L1.integral`, `SimpleFunc.integral_L1_eq_integral`, `MeasureTheory.integral`, `norm_integral_le_integral_norm`).* Taking $F = E$ with `[CompleteSpace E]` and $T = \mathrm{weightedSMul}\,\mu$ ($C = 1$) defines `L1.integralCLM : (α →₁[μ] E) →L[ℝ] E` and `L1.integral` with $\lVert \mathrm{L1.integralCLM}\rVert \le 1$ and $\lVert \mathrm{L1.integral}\,f\rVert_E \le \lVert f\rVert_{L^1}$. On every integrable simple class $[s] \in \alpha \to_{1s}[\mu] E$ (and via `setToFun_simpleFunc` on every raw integrable simple function $s : \alpha \to_s E$), it recovers the finite sum $\sum_{x \in s.\mathrm{range}} \mu.\mathrm{real}(s^{-1}\{x\}) \cdot x$, and it supplies linearity, $\mathbb K$-homogeneity, a.e. congruence, $\lVert \int a,\,g(a)\,d\mu\rVert_E \le \int a,\,\lVert g(a)\rVert_E\,d\mu$, and the $L^1$-continuity lemma `tendsto_setToL1` used in §2.3.2.7. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/FinMeasAdditive.lean:188–194,390–498,582–597`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/FinMeasAdditive.lean#L390-L498) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean:88–290,398–656,737–852`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/SetToL1.lean#L398-L656) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/L1.lean:77–139,418–616`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/L1.lean#L418-L616) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/Basic.lean:156–320,904–971`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/Basic.lean#L156-L320)

**Proposition 2.3.2.7e (strict non-existence of continuous extension when the target is incomplete).** [Expository] Equip $([0, 1], \mathcal B([0, 1]), \mathrm{vol})$ with Lebesgue measure, and let $F = c_{00}(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ be the normed $\mathbb R$-space of finitely supported real sequences equipped with either $\lVert u\rVert_\infty = \sup_{m \ge 1} \lvert u_m\rvert$ or $\lVert u\rVert_1 = \sum_{m=1}^\infty \lvert u_m\rvert$. Then:

1. The simple-function Bochner integral $I_0 := \mathrm{L1.SimpleFunc.integralCLM} : ([0, 1] \to_{1s}[\mathrm{vol}] F) \to_L F$ is a well-defined continuous $\mathbb R$-linear map of exact operator norm $1$.
2. Setting $D_n := (2^{-n}, 2^{-n+1}]$ ($n \ge 1$), the dyadic basis map $h(x) := \sum_{n=1}^\infty e_n\,1_{D_n}(x)$ ($h(0) := 0$) takes every value in $F$, is Borel strongly measurable and integrable with $\lVert [h]\rVert_{L^1([0,1]; F)} = 1$, and its simple truncations $h_N := \sum_{n=1}^N e_n\,1_{D_n}$ satisfy $\lVert [h] - [h_N]\rVert_{L^1([0,1]; F)} = 2^{-N} \to 0$.
3. **No continuous map $G : ([0, 1] \to_1[\mathrm{vol}] F) \to F$ (linear or nonlinear) satisfies $G([s]) = I_0([s])$ for every $[s] \in ([0, 1] \to_{1s}[\mathrm{vol}] F)$.**

---

**Proofs.**

*Proof of Lemma 2.3.2.7a.* We give both the sequential Cauchy proof on normed spaces and the general filter proof implemented in `UniformEmbedding.lean:440–501` and `DenseEmbedding.lean:126–168`.

- *Sequential proof (when $\alpha, \beta, \gamma$ carry norms/metrics with $e$ linear, $\lVert x\rVert_\beta \le N\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_\alpha$, and $\lVert f(x) - f(x')\rVert_\gamma \le M\,\lVert x - x'\rVert_\beta$).* Let $a \in \alpha$. Since $e(\beta)$ is dense in $\alpha$, there exists a sequence $(x_n)_{n \ge 1}$ in $\beta$ with $\lVert e(x_n) - a\rVert_\alpha \to 0$. Then $(e(x_n))$ is Cauchy in $\alpha$, so $\lVert x_m - x_n\rVert_\beta \le N\,\lVert e(x_m) - e(x_n)\rVert_\alpha \to 0$ as $m, n \to \infty$; hence $(x_n)$ is Cauchy in $\beta$. Uniform continuity gives $\lVert f(x_m) - f(x_n)\rVert_\gamma \le M\,\lVert x_m - x_n\rVert_\beta \to 0$, so $(f(x_n))$ is Cauchy in $\gamma$. By completeness of the **target** $\gamma$ (notice that completeness of $\alpha$ is never used, because $a \in \alpha$ is already given!), $(f(x_n))$ converges to a limit $L \in \gamma$, unique because $\gamma$ is Hausdorff. If $(x'_n)_{n \ge 1}$ is any second sequence in $\beta$ with $e(x'_n) \to a$, then $\lVert e(x_n - x'_n)\rVert_\alpha \le \lVert e(x_n) - a\rVert_\alpha + \lVert a - e(x'_n)\rVert_\alpha \to 0$, hence $\lVert x_n - x'_n\rVert_\beta \le N\,\lVert e(x_n - x'_n)\rVert_\alpha \to 0$, and therefore $\lVert f(x_n) - f(x'_n)\rVert_\gamma \le M\,\lVert x_n - x'_n\rVert_\beta \to 0$, proving $\lim_{n \to \infty} f(x'_n) = L$. Finally, when $a = e(b)$, the constant sequence $x_n = b$ satisfies $e(x_n) = e(b) \to a$, so independence of the representative sequence forces $L = \lim_{n \to \infty} f(b) = f(b)$.

- *Filter proof in pinned Mathlib (`uniformly_extend_exists`, `uniformly_extend_spec`, `uniformly_extend_of_ind`).* Fix $a \in \alpha$ and set $de := h_e.\mathrm{isDenseInducing}\,h_{\mathrm{dense}}$. First, $\mathcal N(a)$ is Cauchy in $\alpha$ (`cauchy_nhds`, `Cauchy.lean:78–80`: every entourage of $\alpha$ contains $V \times V$ for a small neighbourhood $V \in \mathcal N(a)$). Second, for every $U \in \mathcal N(a)$, density of $e(\beta)$ furnishes $x \in \beta$ with $e(x) \in U$ (`mem_closure_iff_nhds`), so $e^{-1}(U)$ is nonempty and $\mathcal F_a := \mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(a))$ satisfies `NeBot` (`de.comap_nhds_neBot a`, `DenseEmbedding.lean:126–130`). Third, because `h_e.comap_uniformity` identifies $\mathrm{comap}\,(e \times e)\,\mathcal U_\alpha = \mathcal U_\beta$, pulling back $\mathcal N(a) \times \mathcal N(a) \le \mathcal U_\alpha$ gives $\mathcal F_a \times \mathcal F_a = \mathrm{comap}\,(e \times e)\,(\mathcal N(a) \times \mathcal N(a)) \le \mathcal U_\beta$, so $\mathcal F_a$ is Cauchy in $\beta$ (`Cauchy.comap'`, `Cauchy.lean:161–174`). Fourth, since `h_f : map (f × f) (𝓤 β) ≤ 𝓤 γ`, pushing forward gives $(\mathrm{map}\,f\,\mathcal F_a) \times (\mathrm{map}\,f\,\mathcal F_a) = \mathrm{map}\,(f \times f)\,(\mathcal F_a \times \mathcal F_a) \le \mathcal U_\gamma$, so $\mathrm{map}\,f\,\mathcal F_a$ is Cauchy in $\gamma$ (`Cauchy.map`, `:153–159`). Applying `CompleteSpace.complete` (`Cauchy.lean:370–373`) yields $c \in \gamma$ with $\mathrm{map}\,f\,\mathcal F_a \le \mathcal N(c)$ (`uniformly_extend_exists`, `UniformEmbedding.lean:440–446`), and Hilbert's $\varepsilon$-specification `tendsto_nhds_limUnder` gives $f \to \psi(a)$ along $\mathcal F_a$ (`uniformly_extend_spec`, `:466–469`).

  For part (2), in a uniform space `[T0Space γ]` implies `[T2Space γ]`. If $x : \mathbb N \to \beta$ satisfies $e(x_n) \to a$, then $\mathrm{map}\,(e \circ x)\,\mathrm{atTop} \le \mathcal N(a)$, which by `map_le_iff_le_comap` is equivalent to $\mathrm{map}\,x\,\mathrm{atTop} \le \mathcal F_a$. Composing with $\mathrm{map}\,f\,\mathcal F_a \le \mathcal N(\psi(a))$ yields $\mathrm{map}\,(f \circ x)\,\mathrm{atTop} \le \mathcal N(\psi(a))$, i.e., $f(x_n) \to \psi(a)$ for *every* approximating sequence simultaneously — no countable choice is needed and representative independence is automatic from uniqueness of Hausdorff limits (`Tendsto.limUnder_eq`, `Hausdorff.lean:292–295`). At $a = e(b)$, `de.nhds_eq_comap b` (`DenseEmbedding.lean:54`) rewrites $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(e(b))) = \mathcal N(b)$, so continuity of $f$ at $b$ (`h_f.continuous.continuousAt`) gives $f \to f(b)$ along $\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(e(b)))$ and hence $\psi(e(b)) = f(b)$ (`extend_eq_at`, `DenseEmbedding.lean:157–159`; `uniformly_extend_of_ind`, `UniformEmbedding.lean:500–501`). $\square$

*Proof of Lemma 2.3.2.7b.* For (1) (`uniformContinuous_uniformly_extend`, `UniformEmbedding.lean:471–496`), let $d \in \mathcal U_\gamma$ be any target entourage. Choose a symmetric entourage $s \in \mathcal U_\gamma$ whose three-fold relational composition satisfies $s \circ s \circ s \subseteq d$ (`comp3_mem_uniformity`, the uniform-space form of $\frac{\varepsilon}{3} + \frac{\varepsilon}{3} + \frac{\varepsilon}{3} = \varepsilon$). First, for any $y \in \alpha$ and any neighbourhood $m \in \mathcal N(y)$, both $f(e^{-1}(m))$ and $\{z \in \gamma \mid (z, \psi(y)) \in s \land (\psi(y), z) \in s\}$ belong to the proper filter $\mathrm{map}\,f\,(\mathrm{comap}\,e\,(\mathcal N(y)))$ by Lemma 2.3.2.7a(1), so their intersection is nonempty (`h_pnt`, lines 473–482): there exists $w \in \beta$ with $e(w) \in m$, $(f(w), \psi(y)) \in s$, and $(\psi(y), f(w)) \in s$. Second, uniform continuity of $f$ gives $(f \times f)^{-1}(s) \in \mathcal U_\beta = \mathrm{comap}\,(e \times e)\,\mathcal U_\alpha$, so there exists $t \in \mathcal U_\alpha$ with $(e \times e)^{-1}(t) \subseteq (f \times f)^{-1}(s)$ (lines 483–486). Pass to the open entourage $\operatorname{int}(t) \in \mathcal U_\alpha$ (`interior_mem_uniformity`, line 488). Whenever $(y_1, y_2) \in \operatorname{int}(t)$, openness supplies $m_1 \in \mathcal N(y_1)$ and $m_2 \in \mathcal N(y_2)$ with $m_1 \times m_2 \subseteq \operatorname{int}(t) \subseteq t$ (`mem_nhds_prod_iff`). Applying `h_pnt` to $m_1$ and $m_2$ produces $w_1, w_2 \in \beta$ such that $(e(w_1), e(w_2)) \in m_1 \times m_2 \subseteq t$ — hence $(f(w_1), f(w_2)) \in s$ — while $(\psi(y_1), f(w_1)) \in s$ and $(f(w_2), \psi(y_2)) \in s$. Composing the three steps in $s \circ s \circ s \subseteq d$ gives $(\psi(y_1), \psi(y_2)) \in d$, proving `UniformContinuous ψ` (and hence `cont : Continuous ψ`).

For (2) (`Extend.lean:58–96`), when `h : DenseRange e ∧ IsUniformInducing e` holds, the `dite` in `ContinuousLinearMap.extend` selects the main branch with underlying function $\psi$, continuous by (1) applied to `f.uniformContinuous`, and satisfying $\psi(e(x)) = f(x)$ (`eq`, line 63; exported as `extend_eq`, lines 86–90). Because $F$ is a `T0Space` uniform additive group, $F$ is Hausdorff (`T2Space`), so for any two continuous maps $\Phi_1, \Phi_2 : X \to F$ on a topological space $X$, the equalizer $\{z \in X \mid \Phi_1(z) = \Phi_2(z)\}$ is closed (`isClosed_eq`).

- *Additivity (`map_add'`, lines 65–71):* Apply `h.1.induction_on₂` (`DenseEmbedding.lean:364–368`, which uses density of $(e \times e)(E \times E)$ in $E_\ell \times E_\ell$) to the predicate $P(y_1, y_2) :\Leftrightarrow \psi(y_1 + y_2) = \psi(y_1) + \psi(y_2)$. Continuity of $\psi$ and of addition in $E_\ell$ and $F$ makes $\{(y_1, y_2) \mid P(y_1, y_2)\}$ closed (`isClosed_eq`), and on dense pairs $(e(x_1), e(x_2))$ we have
  $$\psi(e(x_1) + e(x_2)) = \psi(e(x_1 + x_2)) = f(x_1 + x_2) = f(x_1) + f(x_2) = \psi(e(x_1)) + \psi(e(x_2)).$$
- *Semilinearity (`map_smul'`, lines 72–79):* For each scalar $k \in \mathbb K$, apply `h.1.induction_on` (`DenseEmbedding.lean:359–361`) to $Q(y) :\Leftrightarrow \psi(k \cdot y) = \sigma_{12}(k) \cdot \psi(y)$. Continuity of $y \mapsto k \cdot y$ in $E_\ell$, of $z \mapsto \sigma_{12}(k) \cdot z$ in $F$, and of $\psi$ makes $\{y \mid Q(y)\}$ closed, and at $y = e(x)$, $\psi(k \cdot e(x)) = \psi(e(k \cdot x)) = f(k \cdot x) = \sigma_{12}(k) \cdot f(x) = \sigma_{12}(k) \cdot \psi(e(x))$. (In sequential terms, if $e(x_n) \to y_1$ and $e(z_n) \to y_2$, then $e(x_n + z_n) \to y_1 + y_2$ and $e(k \cdot x_n) \to k \cdot y_1$, so continuity of $+$ and $\cdot$ in $F$ passes linearity to the limit.)
- *Uniqueness (`extend_unique`, lines 91–95):* If $g : E_\ell \to_{SL[\sigma_{12}]} F$ satisfies $g \circ e = f$, then $\psi$ and $g$ are continuous maps into the Hausdorff space $F$ agreeing on the dense set $e(E)$, so `uniformly_extend_unique` (`UniformEmbedding.lean:503–504` $\to$ `IsDenseInducing.extend_unique`, `DenseEmbedding.lean:188–190`; equivalently `DenseRange.equalizer`, `:381–383`) gives $\psi = g$, and `coeFn_injective` lifts pointwise equality to `f.extend e = g`. $\square$

*Proof of Theorem 2.3.2.7c.* For (1), given $\forall x \in E,\ \lVert x\rVert_E \le N\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}$, for all $x, x' \in E$ we have $\lVert x - x'\rVert_E \le N\,\lVert e(x - x')\rVert_{E_\ell} = N\,\lVert e(x) - e(x')\rVert_{E_\ell}$, so $e$ is `AntilipschitzWith N` (`AddMonoidHomClass.antilipschitz_of_bound`, `Group/Uniform.lean:135–138`). Pairing `AntilipschitzWith N e` with `e.uniformContinuous` yields `IsUniformEmbedding e` (`isUniformEmbedding_of_bound`, `ContinuousLinearMap.lean:157–159`), and in particular `IsUniformInducing e`. Next, `opNorm_extend_le` (`Extend.lean:117–134`) invokes `opNorm_le_bound` (`Operator/Basic.lean:199–201`): since $0 \le N$ (`N : ℝ≥0`) and $0 \le \lVert f\rVert$, we have $0 \le N\,\lVert f\rVert$ (lines 121–127 also guard the vacuous negative branch via `Unique E`). It therefore suffices to show $\lVert (f.\mathrm{extend}\,e)(y)\rVert_F \le N\,\lVert f\rVert\,\lVert y\rVert_{E_\ell}$ for every $y \in E_\ell$. Because both $y \mapsto \lVert (f.\mathrm{extend}\,e)(y)\rVert_F$ and $y \mapsto N\,\lVert f\rVert\,\lVert y\rVert_{E_\ell}$ are continuous into $\mathbb R$, the set $\{y \in E_\ell \mid \lVert (f.\mathrm{extend}\,e)(y)\rVert_F \le N\,\lVert f\rVert\,\lVert y\rVert_{E_\ell}\}$ is closed (`isClosed_le`). By `isClosed_property h_dense` (`DenseEmbedding.lean:337–345`), it suffices to check $y = e(x)$ ($x \in E$), where Lemma 2.3.2.7b(2) (`extend_eq`) and `le_opNorm` (`Operator/Basic.lean:237`) give
$$\lVert (f.\mathrm{extend}\,e)(e(x))\rVert_F = \lVert f(x)\rVert_F \le \lVert f\rVert\,\lVert x\rVert_E \le \lVert f\rVert\,(N\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}) = (N\,\lVert f\rVert)\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}.$$
When $e$ is an isometry ($\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell} = \lVert x\rVert_E$), taking $N = 1$ gives $\lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert \le \lVert f\rVert$, while for every $x \in E$, $\lVert f(x)\rVert_F = \lVert (f.\mathrm{extend}\,e)(e(x))\rVert_F \le \lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell} = \lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert\,\lVert x\rVert_E$ gives $\lVert f\rVert \le \lVert f.\mathrm{extend}\,e\rVert$ by `opNorm_le_bound`.

For (2) (`Extend.lean:155–232`), the bound $\lVert f(x)\rVert_F \le C\,\lVert e(x)\rVert_{E_\ell}$ forces $f(x) = 0$ whenever $e(x) = 0$ (in particular $\ker e \subseteq \ker f$). Hence $f$ descends to $E/\ker e \simeq_\ell \operatorname{range}(e)$ (`(ker e).liftQ f` composed with `e.quotKerEquivRange.symm`) with bound $\lVert \bar f(z)\rVert_F \le C\,\lVert z\rVert_{E_\ell}$ on $\operatorname{range}(e) \subseteq E_\ell$, forming `f.compLeftInverse e : range e →SL[σ₁₂] F` via `mkContinuousOfExistsBound` (lines 155–170). Extending along the isometric subtype inclusion `(range e).subtypeL` (`isUniformEmbedding_subtype_val`, $N = 1$) gives `(f.extendOfNorm e) (e x) = f x` (`extendOfNorm_eq`, lines 194–200), transfers $\lVert (f.\mathrm{extendOfNorm}\,e)(y)\rVert_F \le C\,\lVert y\rVert_{E_\ell}$ from $\operatorname{range}(e)$ to all $y \in E_\ell$ by `h_dense.induction` and `isClosed_le` (`norm_extendOfNorm_apply_le`, lines 201–208), and concludes $\lVert f.\mathrm{extendOfNorm}\,e\rVert \le C$ via `opNorm_le_bound` (`opNorm_extendOfNorm_le`, lines 229–232). $\square$

*Proof of Theorem 2.3.2.7d.*

1. *Well-definedness, linearity, and norm on simple $L^1$ classes (`FinMeasAdditive.lean:188–194, 390–498, 582–597`, `SetToL1.lean:88–345`).* First, if $s$ is measurable with $\mu(s) = 0$, `DominatedFinMeasAdditive` gives $\lVert T(s)\rVert \le C\,\mu.\mathrm{real}(s) = C \cdot 0 = 0$, so $T(s) = 0$ (`hT.eq_zero_of_measure_zero`, `FinMeasAdditive.lean:188–194`). If $s_1, s_2 : \alpha \to_s E$ are integrable simple functions with $s_1 =^{\mathrm{ae}}_\mu s_2$, then for every pair of distinct values $v \neq w$ in $E$, the measurable intersection $s_1^{-1}\{v\} \cap s_2^{-1}\{w\}$ lies inside $\{a \mid s_1(a) \neq s_2(a)\}$ and hence has $\mu$-measure $0$, so $T(s_1^{-1}\{v\} \cap s_2^{-1}\{w\}) = 0$; refining both fiber decompositions over the product simple function `pair s_1 s_2` gives `s_1.setToSimpleFunc T = s_2.setToSimpleFunc T` (`setToSimpleFunc_congr`, `:371–400`). Consequently `setToL1S T f := (toSimpleFunc f).setToSimpleFunc T` depends only on the $\mu$-a.e. class $f \in \alpha \to_{1s}[\mu] E$ (`setToL1S_congr`, `SetToL1.lean:127–131`), and finite additivity on `pair` transfers additivity and $\mathbb R$-homogeneity (`setToL1S_add`, `setToL1S_smul_real`, `:168–198`). For the norm bound (`norm_eq_sum_mul`, `:88–102`; `norm_setToL1S_le`, `:208–215`), write $s := \mathrm{toSimpleFunc}\,f$; every nonzero value $x \in s.\mathrm{range}$ has $\mu(s^{-1}\{x\}) < \infty$ by integrability, while $T(s^{-1}\{0\})(0) = 0$, so
   $$\lVert \mathrm{setToL1S}\,T\,f\rVert_F = \left\lVert \sum_{x \in s.\mathrm{range}} T(s^{-1}\{x\})(x)\right\rVert_F \le \sum_{x \in s.\mathrm{range}} \lVert T(s^{-1}\{x\})\rVert\,\lVert x\rVert_E \le C \sum_{x \in s.\mathrm{range}} \mu.\mathrm{real}(s^{-1}\{x\})\,\lVert x\rVert_E = C\,\lVert f\rVert_{L^1_s}.$$
   Applying `LinearMap.mkContinuous` produces `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT` with norm $\le C$ if $C \ge 0$ (`mkContinuous_norm_le`, `SetToL1.lean:338–340`) and $\le \max(C, 0)$ unconditionally (`mkContinuous_norm_le'`, `:342–345`).

2. *Isometric $L^1$ extension and $\mathbb K$-scalar upgrade (`SimpleFuncDenseLp.lean:643–685`, `SetToL1.lean:398–617`).* By `isUniformEmbedding_comap Subtype.val_injective`, the subgroup coercion `coeToLp α E ℝ : (α →₁ₛ[μ] E) →L[ℝ] (α →₁[μ] E)` satisfies `simpleFunc.isUniformInducing` (`SimpleFuncDenseLp.lean:643–647`) and $\lVert \mathrm{coeToLp}\,f\rVert_{L^1} = \lVert f\rVert_{L^1_s}$, while Chapter 14 ([§14.4.2.3.1](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#144231-finite-measure-simple-functions-in-lp)) supplies `simpleFunc.denseRange one_ne_top` (`:671–673`). Under `[CompleteSpace F]`, Lemma 2.3.2.7b(2) applied to `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT` and `coeToLp α E ℝ` yields `L1.setToL1 hT`, its agreement `setToL1_eq_setToL1SCLM` (`SetToL1.lean:421–424`), and uniqueness `setToL1_unique` (`:430–439`). Applying Theorem 2.3.2.7c(1) at **`N = (1 : ℝ≥0)`** (`norm_setToL1_le_norm_setToL1SCLM`, `:576–586`) gives
   $$\lVert \mathrm{L1.setToL1}\,hT\rVert \le 1 \cdot \lVert \mathrm{setToL1SCLM}\,\alpha\,E\,\mu\,hT\rVert \le \max(C, 0)$$
   (`norm_setToL1_le'`, `:605–607`, and $\le C$ when $C \ge 0$, `norm_setToL1_le`, `:602–603`). When a normed ring $\mathbb K$ acts boundedly on $E$ and $F$ and $T(s)(c \cdot x) = c \cdot T(s)(x)$, `setToL1' 𝕜 hT h_smul` (`:399–401`) extends the $\mathbb K$-linear map `setToL1SCLM'` along `coeToLp α E 𝕜`; because `L1.setToL1 hT` and `setToL1' 𝕜 hT h_smul` are both continuous on $\alpha \to_1[\mu] E$ into the Hausdorff space $F$ and both equal `setToL1SCLM α E μ hT` on the dense range of `coeToLp`, `Dense.induction` + `isClosed_eq` proves `setToL1 hT f = setToL1' 𝕜 hT h_smul f` for all $f \in \alpha \to_1[\mu] E$ (`setToL1_eq_setToL1'`, `:441–449`), whence $\mathbb K$-homogeneity `setToL1_smul` (`:503–508`).

3. *Bochner integral and function-level consumers (`Bochner/L1.lean:77–139, 529–616`, `Bochner/Basic.lean:156–320, 904–949`).* For `weightedSMul μ s = μ.real s • id_E`, finite additivity of $\mu.\mathrm{real}$ on disjoint finite-measure measurable sets (`weightedSMul_union`, `Bochner/L1.lean:118–121`) and $\lVert \mu.\mathrm{real}(s)\cdot \mathrm{id}_E\rVert = \mu.\mathrm{real}(s)\,\lVert \mathrm{id}_E\rVert \le 1 \cdot \mu.\mathrm{real}(s)$ (`norm_weightedSMul_le`, `:127–135`, using `norm_id_le`, `Operator/Basic.lean:228`) give `dominatedFinMeasAdditive_weightedSMul μ` with $C = 1$. Since `L1.SimpleFunc.integralCLM` is definitionally `setToL1SCLM` for `weightedSMul μ`, its extension `L1.integralCLM` (`Bochner/L1.lean:529–545`) coincides with `L1.setToL1 (dominatedFinMeasAdditive_weightedSMul μ)`, giving $\lVert \mathrm{L1.integralCLM}\rVert \le 1$ (`norm_Integral_le_one`, `:596–597`), agreement on simple classes (`SimpleFunc.integral_L1_eq_integral`, `:548–552`), and $\mathbb K$-homogeneity via `L1.integral_eq' 𝕜` (`:560–595`). Passing through `integral_eq_setToFun` (`Bochner/Basic.lean:190–196`) transfers `setToFun_add`, `setToFun_smul`, `setToFun_congr_ae`, `setToFun_simpleFunc`, and `tendsto_setToL1` directly to `MeasureTheory.integral`, while `L1.norm_of_fun_eq_integral_norm` (`Bochner/Basic.lean:915–918`) rewrites $\lVert h_f.\mathrm{toL1}\,f\rVert_{L^1} = \int a,\,\lVert f(a)\rVert_E\,d\mu$ to deliver `norm_integral_le_integral_norm` (`:934–943`). $\square$

*Proof of Proposition 2.3.2.7e.* Let $\lVert\cdot\rVert$ denote either $\lVert\cdot\rVert_\infty$ or $\lVert\cdot\rVert_1$ on $F = c_{00}(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$; under both norms, $\lVert e_n\rVert = 1$ for every standard basis vector $e_n$ ($n \ge 1$), and every coordinate functional $\pi_k : F \to \mathbb R$, $\pi_k(u) := u_k$, satisfies $\lvert \pi_k(u) - \pi_k(w)\rvert \le \lVert u - w\rVert$ and is therefore continuous.

1. *Well-defined norm-$1$ simple integral:* Neither `L1.SimpleFunc.integralCLM` (`Bochner/L1.lean:446–463`) nor `Lp F 1 vol` (`LpSpace/Basic.lean:89–108`) assumes `[CompleteSpace F]`: for any $[s] \in [0, 1] \to_{1s}[\mathrm{vol}] F$, $I_0([s]) = \sum_{v \in (\mathrm{toSimpleFunc}\,s).\mathrm{range}} \mathrm{vol}.\mathrm{real}(s^{-1}\{v\}) \cdot v$ is a *finite* linear combination of elements of $c_{00}$, hence belongs to $c_{00}$ with $\lVert I_0([s])\rVert \le \lVert [s]\rVert_{L^1}$, and $[e_1\,1_{[0,1]}]$ attains $\lVert I_0([e_1\,1_{[0,1]}])\rVert = \lVert e_1\rVert = 1 = \lVert [e_1\,1_{[0,1]}]\rVert_{L^1}$.
2. *Membership of $[h]$ in $L^1([0, 1]; c_{00})$ and simple convergence:* For $x = 0$, $h(0) = 0 \in c_{00}$; for $x \in (0, 1]$, $x$ lies in a unique dyadic interval $D_n = (2^{-n}, 2^{-n+1}]$, where $h(x) = e_n \in c_{00}$ and $h_N(x) = e_n = h(x)$ for all $N \ge n$. Thus $h_N(x) \to h(x)$ in $F$ at *every* $x \in [0, 1]$, proving `StronglyMeasurable h`, and $\int_0^1 \lVert h(x)\rVert\,dx = \sum_{n=1}^\infty \lVert e_n\rVert\,\mathrm{vol}(D_n) = \sum_{n=1}^\infty 2^{-n} = 1 < \infty$, proving `Integrable h vol` with $\lVert [h]\rVert_{L^1} = 1$. Moreover, $h - h_N = \sum_{n=N+1}^\infty e_n\,1_{D_n}$ has exact $L^1$ norm $\lVert [h] - [h_N]\rVert_{L^1} = \sum_{n=N+1}^\infty 2^{-n} = 2^{-N} \to 0$, so $[h_N] \to [h]$ in $[0, 1] \to_1[\mathrm{vol}] F$.
3. *Non-existence of any continuous extension:* Suppose for a contradiction that $G : ([0, 1] \to_1[\mathrm{vol}] F) \to F$ is a continuous map (not even assumed linear) satisfying $G([s]) = I_0([s])$ for all $[s] \in [0, 1] \to_{1s}[\mathrm{vol}] F$. Since $[h_N] \to [h]$ in $L^1([0, 1]; F)$, continuity of $G$ at $[h]$ implies $I_0([h_N]) = G([h_N]) \to G([h])$ in $(F, \lVert\cdot\rVert)$. Evaluating $I_0([h_N])$ gives
   $$I_0([h_N]) = \sum_{n=1}^N \mathrm{vol}(D_n)\,e_n = \sum_{n=1}^N 2^{-n} e_n \in c_{00}$$
   (which is Cauchy under both norms, with $\lVert I_0([h_M]) - I_0([h_N])\rVert_\infty = 2^{-(N+1)}$ and $\lVert I_0([h_M]) - I_0([h_N])\rVert_1 = 2^{-N} - 2^{-M}$ for $M > N$). Fix *any* coordinate index $k \ge 1$. For every $N \ge k$, $\pi_k(I_0([h_N])) = 2^{-k}$. By continuity of $\pi_k : F \to \mathbb R$,
   $$(G([h]))_k = \pi_k(G([h])) = \lim_{N \to \infty} \pi_k(I_0([h_N])) = 2^{-k} \neq 0 \qquad \text{for every } k \ge 1.$$
   Hence $G([h])$ has infinitely many nonzero coordinates, contradicting $G([h]) \in c_{00}(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$. $\square$

**Remarks (hypothesis boundaries, why `CompleteSpace (α →₁[μ] E)` is separate, and historical attribution).**

1. *What happens in Mathlib when `[CompleteSpace F]` fails, and why `CompleteSpace (α →₁[μ] E)` is a separate theorem.* [Expository] Proposition 2.3.2.7e shows that when `¬CompleteSpace F`, no continuous extension from $L^1_s(\mu; F)$ to $L^1(\mu; F)$ can exist in general. In `SetToL1.lean:396` and `Bochner/L1.lean:519`, `L1.setToL1` and `L1.integral` carry `[CompleteSpace F]` / `[CompleteSpace E]` as a typeclass requirement, so they cannot even be invoked for $F = c_{00}$. At the unbundled function level, `setToFun` (`SetToL1.lean:631`) and `MeasureTheory.integral` (`Bochner/Basic.lean:157`) test `if CompleteSpace G then ... else 0` (`integral_of_not_completeSpace`, `:213–215`). Thus for $F = c_{00}$, `∫ x, h_N x = 0` for every $N$, even though $\mathrm{SimpleFunc.integral}\,\mathrm{vol}\,h_N = \sum_{n=1}^N 2^{-n} e_n \neq 0$: agreement between `MeasureTheory.integral` and `SimpleFunc.integral` (`setToFun_simpleFunc`, `SetToL1.lean:837`) requires `[CompleteSpace F]`. Conversely, notice that neither `ContinuousLinearMap.extend` nor `L1.integral` ever invokes completeness of the *domain* $L^1(\mu; E)$: in Proposition 2.3.2.7e, $L^1([0, 1]; c_{00})$ is itself incomplete, yet $[h]$ already exists as a point of $L^1([0, 1]; c_{00})$ with $[h_N] \to [h]$. Riesz–Fischer completeness `Lp.instCompleteSpace : CompleteSpace (Lp E p μ)` (`LpSpace/Complete.lean:378`, retained here as a named `[Lean]` input for $1 \le p \le \infty$ and `[CompleteSpace E]`) is needed only when an operator **takes values in $L^p$** — as `condExpL1CLM : (α →₁[μ] F') →L[ℝ] (α →₁[μ] F')` does in row 5 of the Consumer Contract Table — or when one constructs a limit function from an $L^p$-Cauchy sequence without a given pointwise limit.

2. *Deleting `IsUniformInducing e` or `DenseRange e` in `ContinuousLinearMap.extend`.* [Expository] Both clauses of the guard `if h : DenseRange e ∧ IsUniformInducing e` in `Extend.lean:59` are load-bearing:
   - *Deleting `IsUniformInducing e` (no finite $N$ in Theorem 2.3.2.7c(1)):* Equip $E = c_{00}(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ with $\lVert u\rVert_1 = \sum_{k \ge 1} \lvert u_k\rvert$, let $E_\ell = \ell^2(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ with $\lVert u\rVert_2 = \bigl(\sum_{k \ge 1} \lvert u_k\rvert^2\bigr)^{1/2}$, and let $e : (c_{00}, \lVert\cdot\rVert_1) \to_L \ell^2$ be the inclusion. Since $\lVert u\rVert_2 \le \lVert u\rVert_1$, $e$ is a continuous injective linear map with `DenseRange e`, and $f : (c_{00}, \lVert\cdot\rVert_1) \to_L \mathbb R$, $f(u) := \sum_{k \ge 1} u_k$, has operator norm $\lVert f\rVert = 1$ into the complete target $F = \mathbb R$. Yet `IsUniformInducing e` fails because $\lVert \sum_{k=1}^N e_k\rVert_1 = N$ while $\lVert \sum_{k=1}^N e_k\rVert_2 = \sqrt{N}$. For the harmonic truncations $u^{(N)} := \sum_{k=1}^N \frac{1}{k} e_k \in c_{00}$, $e(u^{(N)})$ converges in $\ell^2$ ($\lVert e(u^{(M)}) - e(u^{(N)})\rVert_2^2 = \sum_{k=N+1}^M k^{-2} < \frac{1}{N} \to 0$; e.g. at $N = 1, 4, 11, 31$, $\lVert e(u^{(N)})\rVert_2^2 = 1, \frac{205}{144}, \frac{239437889}{153679680}, \dots < \frac{\pi^2}{6} < 2$), whereas $f(u^{(N)}) = H_N = 1, \frac{25}{12}, \frac{83711}{27720}, \dots \to +\infty$. Thus $(f(u^{(N)}))$ is not Cauchy in $\mathbb R$, no continuous extension $\ell^2 \to \mathbb R$ exists, and `f.extend e` falls back to $0$.
   - *Deleting `DenseRange e` breaks uniqueness (`extend_unique`):* Let $e : \mathbb R \to_L (\mathbb R^2, \lVert\cdot\rVert_1)$, $e(t) := (t, 0)$, which is an isometric embedding ($N = 1$, `IsUniformInducing e`) whose range $\mathbb R \times \{0\}$ is not dense in $\mathbb R^2$, and let $f = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb R} : \mathbb R \to_L \mathbb R$. Every functional $g_\alpha(x, y) := x + \alpha y$ ($\alpha \in [-1, 1]$) satisfies $g_\alpha \circ e = f$ with $\lVert g_\alpha\rVert = \max(1, \lvert\alpha\rvert) = 1$, so uniqueness fails; `f.extend e` again returns $0$.

3. *Classical origins: Bochner (1933), Bourbaki, and the BLT theorem.* [Expository] In Salomon Bochner's original paper "Integration von Funktionen, deren Werte die Elemente eines Vektorraumes sind", *Fundamenta Mathematicae* 20 (1933), 262–276 (read directly in the ICM scan), §1 (pp. 262–263) postulates from the outset a **complete** complex normed vector space $\mathfrak f$, §2.I (p. 263) defines measurability as almost-everywhere convergence of *endlichwertige Funktionen* (finite-valued simple functions), and §3.II–IV (pp. 265–267) defines $I(g, A) = \sum_{\nu=1}^n \gamma_\nu\,mA_\nu$ on finite-valued functions, proves Cauchy convergence of $I(f_n, A)$ under a summable real majorant $S(x)$ by combining Egorov uniform subsets inside a finite cube $W$ with small-measure and exterior-tail bounds (§3.III *Hilfssatz*, p. 266), truncates simple approximants $g_n^*(x)$ against $G(x) > \lvert f(x)\rvert$, and proves representative-sequence independence on p. 267 by **interleaving** two approximating sequences $g_n, h_n$ into $k_{2n-1} = g_n$, $k_{2n} = h_n$ under $K = G + H$ (observing $L^1$ mean approximation $\int \lvert f - \varphi\rvert\,dx \le \varepsilon$ afterward in §6.I, p. 272). Modern functional analysis factors Bochner's construction into two orthogonal halves: (i) the general uniform-space Cauchy extension theorem (N. Bourbaki, *Elements of Mathematics: General Topology*, Ch. II, §3, no. 6, beginning on p. 190, with the proposed Theorem 2 citation on pp. 190–191 marked [Open] in the [source register](../sources/SOURCES.md#ch02-banach-extension-sources)) and its normed-space specialization, the Bounded Linear Transformation (BLT) theorem (M. Reed and B. Simon, *Methods of Modern Mathematical Physics I: Functional Analysis*, Academic Press, 1980, Theorem I.7, p. 9), implemented in `UniformEmbedding.lean` and `Operator/Extend.lean`; and (ii) density of integrable simple classes in $L^1(\mu; E)$ (Chapter 14, [§14.4.2.3.1](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#144231-finite-measure-simple-functions-in-lp)), which lets `SetToL1.lean` construct the Bochner integral, conditional expectation, and vector-measure integration from a single call to `ContinuousLinearMap.extend`.

**From Hölder to the integral.** [Integral Hölder](#integral-holder-normalization) and
[weighted Hölder](#finite-weighted-holder) give scalar moment estimates.
The [pointwise product theorem](#holder-lp-product) uses the exponent-one case to supply
`Integrable`, through `MemLp.integrable_mul`. Theorem 2.3.2.7d then gives the actual Bochner
integral, its norm inequality and the linearity used by `integral_sub` for the two quotient
integrands. The [metric-valued limit theorem](#metric-valued-ae-limits) supplies the measurable
limit in the separate dominated-convergence argument.

<a id="lp-riesz-fischer-completeness"></a>

##### 2.3.2.7.2 Riesz–Fischer completeness of Bochner $L^p$ spaces

**Motivation.** In §2.3.2.7 and [§2.3.2.7.1](#banach-dense-extension), constructing the Bochner integral `L1.integral` on $L^1(\mu; E)$ via `ContinuousLinearMap.extend` required completeness only of the **codomain** $E$, leaving Riesz–Fischer completeness `Lp.instCompleteSpace : CompleteSpace (Lp E p μ)` (`Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean:378`) as a named `[Lean]` input. As soon as an operator **takes values in an $L^p$ space** — or a closed-graph, Hilbert-projection, or vector-valued Bochner argument targets $L^p$ — completeness of `Lp E p μ` itself becomes load-bearing:

1. in row 5 of §2.3.2.7.1's Consumer Contract Table, `condExpL1CLM` (`CondexpL1.lean:321–324`) invokes `L1.setToL1` with codomain $F = (\alpha \to_1[\mu] F')$, which requires `[CompleteSpace (Lp F' 1 μ)]` synthesized from `[CompleteSpace F']` by `Lp.instCompleteSpace`;
2. `lpMeasSubgroup` and `lpMeas` (`ConditionalExpectation/AEMeasurable.lean:293–310`) inherit `[CompleteSpace]` and closedness `isClosed_aestronglyMeasurable` from `CompleteSpace (Lp F p (μ.trim hm))` along an isometric equivalence, supplying the Hilbert orthogonal projection `condExpL2` (`CondexpL2.lean:68–71`);
3. in the Alpöge–Claude archive, `convSpec` (`ScatteringLimits/ConvolutionSpectra/ConvSpec.lean:24–29`) forms an $L^2$-valued Bochner convolution integral `∫ t, (κ t).compLpL 2 volume (sh V t u)` in `L W = (ℝ →₂[volume] W)`, where `Lp.instCompleteSpace` supplies the complete target needed for Bochner integration, while `BridgeCSchur.lean:208–213` applies the Banach closed-graph theorem into `Lp ℂ 2 μ` alongside `Lp.tendsto_Lp_iff_tendsto_eLpNorm'`.

Opening `Complete.lean:27–382` and its direct suppliers at pinned Mathlib commit `0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474` reveals four structural features that are sharper than many textbook accounts:

- **No global measure or separability assumptions:** $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ is an *arbitrary* measure space (not assumed finite, $\sigma$-finite, or complete) and $E$ is an *arbitrary* complete normed additive commutative group (`[NormedAddCommGroup E] [CompleteSpace E]`, not assumed separable or finite-dimensional).
- **Separate pointwise engines for $1 \le p < \infty$ and $p = \infty$, unified $L^p$-limit engine:** along a controlled Cauchy sequence ($\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n - f_m, p, \mu) < B_N$ for $N \le n, m$ with $\sum_{i=0}^\infty B_i < \infty$), the finite-exponent branch $1 \le p < \infty$ applies [integral Minkowski](#integral-minkowski-supply) to bound partial sums of $\lVert f_{i+1} - f_i\rVert$ by Minkowski's inequality `eLpNorm'_sum_le`, passes to the infinite series via Fatou's lemma `lintegral_liminf_le'`, and extracts a conull finiteness set by Markov contradiction `ae_lt_top'`; the $p = \infty$ branch intersects countably many `ae_lt_of_essSup_lt` sets indexed by $\{(N, n, m) \in \mathbb N^3 : N \le n \land N \le m\}$ via `ae_all_iff` into a single common conull set on which $(f_n(x))$ is uniformly Cauchy in $E$. Both branches then construct a **globally `StronglyMeasurable`** limit $f_{\lim} : \alpha \to E$ (`exists_stronglyMeasurable_limit_of_tendsto_ae`, `AEStronglyMeasurable.lean:711–724`) using per-sequence separable range closure rather than global separability of $E$, and prove $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n - f_{\lim}, p, \mu) \to 0$ (`cauchy_tendsto_of_tendsto`, `:321–343`) directly from lower-semicontinuity of `eLpNorm` — **never** substituting pointwise convergence for $L^p$ convergence, and avoiding dominated convergence (which has no bounded integrable majorant at $p = \infty$).
- **Filter-general geometric extraction and return:** `completeSpace_lp_of_cauchy_complete_eLpNorm` (`:156–190`) delegates subsequence extraction and return to `Metric.complete_of_convergent_controlled_sequences` (`MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean:41–47`) and `UniformSpace.complete_of_convergent_controlled_sequences` (`UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean:904–914`) at geometric gauge $B_n = 2^{-n}$.
- **Sharp hypothesis boundaries:** `completeSpace_of_completeSpace_Lp` (`CompleteOfCompleteLp.lean:80–120`) proves the converse that completeness of a nontrivial `Lp E p μ` forces `[CompleteSpace E]`, while for $0 < p < 1$ Minkowski's constant $1$ inflates to `LpAddConst p = 2 ^ (1 / p.toReal - 1) > 1` (`MeanInequalitiesPow.lean:323`, `TriangleInequality.lean:37–75`), breaking summable-`eLpNorm'` telescoping even though $L^p$ remains complete under the $p$-metric $d_p(f, g) = \int \lVert f - g\rVert^p\,d\mu$.

Simple-function $L^p$ density (`Lp.simpleFunc.denseRange`, Chapter 14, [§14.4.2.3.1](14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#144231-finite-measure-simple-functions-in-lp)), dense continuous linear extension ([§2.3.2.7.1](#banach-dense-extension)), Bochner dominated convergence (§2.3.2.7), monotone convergence (§2.3.2.6), and pseudometrizable a.e.-limit measurability ([§2.3.2.8](#metric-valued-ae-limits)) are cited directly without re-proof.

---

**Examples (two complete hand calculations on infinite measure spaces).** We trace two explicit Cauchy sequences through every stage of the Riesz–Fischer proof: first, a scalar sequence on $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$ ($\mu((0, \infty)) = \infty$) across $p = 1$, $p = 2$, and $p = \infty$ whose un-thinned consecutive differences diverge harmonically and whose raw representatives blow up on a countable null set; second, an infinite-dimensional, **non-separable** $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$-valued sequence on $([0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$, fed directly into `condExpL1CLM`.

1. *Hand calculation 1: scalar $L^1$, $L^2$, and $L^\infty$ on $((0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$ ($\mu((0, \infty)) = \infty$), necessary subsequence extraction, null-set patching, and return to all indices.* [Expository] Equip $X = (0, \infty)$ with Lebesgue measure $\mu = \mathrm{vol}|_{(0, \infty)}$ ($\mu(X) = \infty$; nonzero constants do not belong to $L^p(X)$ for $1 \le p < \infty$), partition $(0, \infty)$ into unit cells $U_j := (j - 1, j]$ ($j \ge 1$, $\mu(U_j) = 1$), and define raw functions $F_m : (0, \infty) \to \mathbb R$ ($m \ge 1$) by
   $$F_m(x) := \sum_{j=1}^m 2^{-j}\,1_{U_j}(x) + \frac{(-1)^m}{m}\,1_{U_1}(x) + m^2\,1_{\mathbb N_{\ge 1}}(x).$$

   - *Why un-thinned consecutive differences fail to telescope.* Off the countable null set $\mathbb N_{\ge 1}$ ($\mu(\mathbb N_{\ge 1}) = 0$), $F_{m+1} - F_m$ equals $(-1)^{m+1}\bigl(\frac{1}{m+1} + \frac{1}{m}\bigr) = (-1)^{m+1}\frac{2m+1}{m(m+1)}$ on $(0, 1)$, equals $2^{-(m+1)}$ on $(m, m+1)$, and vanishes elsewhere. Because $\frac{2m+1}{m(m+1)} > \frac{1}{m+1} \ge 2^{-(m+1)}$ for all $m \ge 1$, the exact consecutive step norms are
     $$\lVert [F_{m+1}] - [F_m]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{2m+1}{m(m+1)} + 2^{-(m+1)} > \frac{2}{m+1}, \qquad \lVert [F_{m+1}] - [F_m]\rVert_{L^2}^2 = \left(\frac{2m+1}{m(m+1)}\right)^2 + 4^{-(m+1)},$$
     $$\lVert [F_{m+1}] - [F_m]\rVert_{L^\infty} = \frac{2m+1}{m(m+1)} > \frac{1}{m+1}.$$
     Hence $\sum_{m=1}^\infty \lVert [F_{m+1}] - [F_m]\rVert_{L^p} = +\infty$ for **all** $p \in \{1, 2, \infty\}$ (by $M = 64$, the partial sum already exceeds $7$ in $L^1$ and $\frac{7}{2}$ in $L^\infty$). Yet $([F_m])_{m \ge 1}$ is Cauchy in $L^1$, $L^2$, and $L^\infty$: for $1 \le r \le m$,
     $$\lVert [F_m] - [F_r]\rVert_{L^1} = \left\lvert\frac{(-1)^m}{m} - \frac{(-1)^r}{r}\right\rvert + \bigl(2^{-r} - 2^{-m}\bigr) \le \frac{2}{r} + 2^{-r} \to 0,$$
     $$\lVert [F_m] - [F_r]\rVert_{L^2}^2 = \left(\frac{(-1)^m}{m} - \frac{(-1)^r}{r}\right)^2 + \frac{4^{-r} - 4^{-m}}{3} \to 0, \qquad \lVert [F_m] - [F_r]\rVert_{L^\infty} \le \frac{2}{r} \to 0.$$

   - *Extracting the geometric dyadic subsequence $k_n := 2^{n+2}$ ($n \ge 0$).* Every $k_n = 4, 8, 16, 32, \dots$ is even, so $(-1)^{k_n} = +1$. For $0 \le N \le n \le m$:
     $$\lVert [F_{k_m}] - [F_{k_n}]\rVert_{L^1} = \bigl(2^{-(n+2)} - 2^{-(m+2)}\bigr) + \bigl(2^{-k_n} - 2^{-k_m}\bigr) < \frac{1}{4}\,2^{-N} + \frac{1}{16}\,2^{-N} = \frac{5}{16}\,2^{-N} < 2^{-N} =: B_N,$$
     $$\lVert [F_{k_m}] - [F_{k_n}]\rVert_{L^2}^2 < 4^{-N} = B_N^2, \qquad \lVert [F_{k_m}] - [F_{k_n}]\rVert_{L^\infty} \le 2^{-(n+2)} - 2^{-(m+2)} < 2^{-N} = B_N,$$
     satisfying `Complete.lean:163`'s exact geometric control $B_N = 2^{-N}$ ($\sum_{N=0}^\infty B_N = 2 < \infty$) simultaneously in $L^1$, $L^2$, and $L^\infty$.

   - *Conull patching, telescoping majorant, and globally Borel limit $f_{\lim}$.* At every integer $j \in \mathbb N_{\ge 1}$, $F_{k_n}(j) \ge k_n^2 = 4^{n+2} \to +\infty$ diverges. Working on the measurable common conull set $\Omega := (0, \infty) \setminus \mathbb N_{\ge 1}$ ($\mu(\Omega^c) = \mu(\mathbb N_{\ge 1}) = 0$) and patching by $0$ on $\mathbb N_{\ge 1}$ defines
     $$f_{\lim}(x) := \sum_{j=1}^\infty 2^{-j}\,1_{(j-1, j)}(x) \qquad (x \in (0, \infty)),$$
     which is Borel `StronglyMeasurable` everywhere on $(0, \infty)$. On $\Omega$, the telescoping absolute-difference series $S(x) := \sum_{n=0}^\infty \lvert F_{k_{n+1}}(x) - F_{k_n}(x)\rvert$ equals $\sum_{n=0}^\infty (2^{-(n+2)} - 2^{-(n+3)}) = \frac{1}{4}$ on $(0, 1)$, $0$ on $(1, 4) \setminus \{2, 3\}$, and $2^{-j}$ on $(j - 1, j)$ for $j \ge 5$, so
     $$\lVert S\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{1}{4} + \sum_{j=5}^\infty 2^{-j} = \frac{5}{16} < 2 = \sum_{N=0}^\infty B_N, \qquad \lVert S\rVert_{L^2}^2 = \frac{1}{16} + \sum_{j=5}^\infty 4^{-j} = \frac{49}{768} < 4 = \left(\sum_{N=0}^\infty B_N\right)^2.$$
     The limit $f_{\lim}$ belongs to `MemLp` across all exponents with $\lVert [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1} = \sum_{j=1}^\infty 2^{-j} = 1$, $\lVert [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^2}^2 = \sum_{j=1}^\infty 4^{-j} = \frac{1}{3}$, and $\lVert [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty} = \sup_{j \ge 1} 2^{-j} = \frac{1}{2}$.

   - *$L^p$ convergence of $(F_{k_n})$ and return to the full sequence $(F_m)_{m \ge 1}$.* For every $m \ge 1$ — both odd and even — $[F_m] - [f_{\lim}]$ equals $\frac{(-1)^m}{m}$ on $(0, 1)$ and $-2^{-j}$ on $(j - 1, j)$ for $j \ge m + 1$, giving exact rational errors (since $\frac{1}{m} > 2^{-(m+1)}$ for all $m \ge 1$):
     $$\lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{1}{m} + 2^{-m} \to 0, \qquad \lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^2}^2 = \frac{1}{m^2} + \frac{4^{-m}}{3} \to 0, \qquad \lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty} = \frac{1}{m} \to 0.$$
     For instance, at odd $m = 15$ via subsequence pivot $k_2 = 16$, $\lVert [F_{15}] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{32783}{491520} \le \bigl(\frac{31}{240} + \frac{1}{65536}\bigr) + \frac{4097}{65536} = \lVert [F_{15}] - [F_{16}]\rVert_{L^1} + \lVert [F_{16}] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1}$:

     | Index $m$ | Role | $\lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^2}^2$ | $\lVert [F_m] - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty}$ |
     |---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
     | $1$ | odd (un-thinned) | $\tfrac{3}{2}$ | $\tfrac{13}{12}$ | $1$ |
     | $3$ | odd (un-thinned) | $\tfrac{11}{24}$ | $\tfrac{67}{576}$ | $\tfrac{1}{3}$ |
     | $4 = k_0$ | dyadic pivot $n = 0$ | $\tfrac{5}{16}$ | $\tfrac{49}{768}$ | $\tfrac{1}{4}$ |
     | $5$ | odd (un-thinned) | $\tfrac{37}{160}$ | $\tfrac{3097}{76800}$ | $\tfrac{1}{5}$ |
     | $8 = k_1$ | dyadic pivot $n = 1$ | $\tfrac{33}{256}$ | $\tfrac{3073}{196608}$ | $\tfrac{1}{8}$ |
     | $15$ | odd return ($m$ near $k_2$) | $\tfrac{32783}{491520}$ | $\tfrac{1073741899}{241591910400}$ | $\tfrac{1}{15}$ |
     | $16 = k_2$ | dyadic pivot $n = 2$ | $\tfrac{4097}{65536}$ | $\tfrac{50331649}{12884901888}$ | $\tfrac{1}{16}$ |
     | $32 = k_3$ | dyadic pivot $n = 3$ | $\tfrac{134217729}{4294967296}$ | $\tfrac{54043195528445953}{55340232221128654848}$ | $\tfrac{1}{32}$ |

2. *Hand calculation 2: infinite-dimensional, non-separable $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$-valued completeness on $([0, \infty), \mathrm{vol})$ and direct evaluation of `condExpL1CLM`.* [Expository] Equip $\alpha = [0, \infty)$ with Borel Lebesgue measure $\mu = \mathrm{vol}|_{[0, \infty)}$ ($\mu(\alpha) = \infty$), and let $\mathcal M_0 \le \mathcal B([0, \infty))$ be the sub-$\sigma$-algebra generated by the unit cells $C_q := [q, q + 1)$ ($q \in \mathbb N$). Although $\mu([0, \infty)) = \infty$, every atom $C_q$ has $\mu(C_q) = 1 < \infty$, so `[SigmaFinite (μ.trim hm)]` holds! Take the **non-separable** Banach space $E = \ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1}; \mathbb R)$ with $\lVert u\rVert_{\ell^\infty} = \sup_{r \ge 1} \lvert u_r\rvert$ (`[CompleteSpace E]`, `¬SeparableSpace E`, since $\{0, 1\}^{\mathbb N_{\ge 1}}$ is an uncountable $1$-separated subset). Split each $C_q$ into $L_q := [q, q + \frac{1}{2})$ and $R_q := [q + \frac{1}{2}, q + 1)$ ($\mu(L_q) = \mu(R_q) = \frac{1}{2}$), define vectors $a^{(q)}, b^{(q)} \in \ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ ($q \ge 0$) by
   $$\bigl(a^{(q)}\bigr)_r := 3^{-q}\,\frac{r}{r + 1}, \qquad \bigl(b^{(q)}\bigr)_r := 3^{-q}\,\frac{1}{r + 1} \qquad (r \ge 1),$$
   and set $G_n(x) := \sum_{q=0}^n \bigl(a^{(q)}\,1_{L_q}(x) + b^{(q)}\,1_{R_q}(x)\bigr)$ ($n \ge 0$).

   - *Separable effective range inside non-separable $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$.* Note that $\lim_{r \to \infty} (a^{(q)})_r = 3^{-q} \neq 0$, so $a^{(q)} \notin c_0(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$, with exact norms $\lVert a^{(q)}\rVert_{\ell^\infty} = \sup_{r \ge 1} 3^{-q}\frac{r}{r+1} = 3^{-q}$ and $\lVert b^{(q)}\rVert_{\ell^\infty} = \sup_{r \ge 1} 3^{-q}\frac{1}{r+1} = \frac{1}{2}\,3^{-q}$ (attained at $r = 1$). Each $G_n$ takes finitely many values ($2n + 3$ values), and the countable union $T := \bigcup_{n=0}^\infty G_n([0, \infty)) = \{0\} \cup \{a^{(q)}, b^{(q)} : q \ge 0\}$ is already closed and countable (hence separable!) inside the non-separable ambient space $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$.
   - *Controlled Cauchy bounds, telescoping limit $G_{\lim}$, and $L^1$ / $L^\infty$ convergence.* On $C_q$, $\int_{C_q} \lVert G_{\lim}(x)\rVert_{\ell^\infty}\,dx = \frac{1}{2}\cdot 3^{-q} + \frac{1}{2}\cdot \frac{1}{2} 3^{-q} = \frac{3}{4}\,3^{-q}$. For $0 \le N \le n < m$:
     $$\lVert [G_m] - [G_n]\rVert_{L^1} = \sum_{q=n+1}^m \frac{3}{4}\,3^{-q} = \frac{3}{8}\bigl(3^{-n} - 3^{-m}\bigr) < \frac{3}{8}\,3^{-N} =: B_N \quad \left(\sum_{N=0}^\infty B_N = \frac{9}{16} < \infty\right),$$
     $$\lVert [G_m] - [G_n]\rVert_{L^\infty} = 3^{-(n+1)} < \frac{1}{2}\,3^{-N} =: B_{\infty, N} \quad \left(\sum_{N=0}^\infty B_{\infty, N} = \frac{3}{4} < \infty\right).$$
     At every $x \in C_q$, $G_n(x)$ stabilizes for $n \ge q$ to $G_{\lim}(x) := \sum_{q=0}^\infty \bigl(a^{(q)}\,1_{L_q}(x) + b^{(q)}\,1_{R_q}(x)\bigr) \in T \subset \ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$, with telescoping $L^1$ majorant $\sum_{n=0}^\infty \lVert [G_{n+1}] - [G_n]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{3}{8} \le \frac{9}{16}$, exact `MemLp` norms $\lVert [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1} = \sum_{q=0}^\infty \frac{3}{4}\,3^{-q} = \frac{9}{8}$ and $\lVert [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty} = 1$, and exact convergence errors $\lVert [G_n] - [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{3}{8}\,3^{-n} \to 0$ and $\lVert [G_n] - [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty} = 3^{-(n+1)} \to 0$.
   - *Feeding $[G_{\lim}]$ into `condExpL1CLM` (`CondexpL1.lean:321`).* Because `Lp.instCompleteSpace` supplies `[CompleteSpace ([0, ∞) →₁[vol] ℓ^∞)]` from `[CompleteSpace ℓ^∞]`, `condExpL1CLM ℓ^∞ hm vol` is a continuous $\mathbb R$-linear contraction on $[0, \infty) \to_1[\mathrm{vol}] \ell^\infty$. On each $\mathcal M_0$-atom $C_q$ ($\mu(C_q) = 1$), `setIntegral_condExpL1CLM` (`CondexpL1.lean:368`) averages over $L_q$ and $R_q$; since $(a^{(q)})_r + (b^{(q)})_r = 3^{-q}\bigl(\frac{r}{r+1} + \frac{1}{r+1}\bigr) = 3^{-q}$ for every $r \ge 1$, writing $\mathbf{1} = (1, 1, 1, \dots) \in \ell^\infty(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ gives
     $$\mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_n]) = \sum_{q=0}^n \frac{a^{(q)} + b^{(q)}}{2}\,1_{C_q} = \sum_{q=0}^n \frac{1}{2}\,3^{-q}\mathbf{1}\,1_{C_q} \xrightarrow{L^1} \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_{\lim}]) = \sum_{q=0}^\infty \frac{1}{2}\,3^{-q}\mathbf{1}\,1_{C_q},$$
     with a strict loss when averaging the two different norm profiles $\bigl\lVert \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_{\lim}])\bigr\rVert_{L^1} = \sum_{q=0}^\infty \frac{1}{2}\,3^{-q} = \frac{3}{4} < \frac{9}{8} = 1 \cdot \lVert [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1}$ and codomain error $\bigl\lVert \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_{\lim}]) - \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_n])\bigr\rVert_{L^1} = \frac{1}{4}\,3^{-n} < \frac{3}{8}\,3^{-n}$:

     | $n$ | $\lVert [G_n] - [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert [G_n] - [G_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^\infty}$ | $\lVert \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_n])\rVert_{L^1}$ | $\lVert \mathrm{condExpL1CLM}([G_{\lim}] - [G_n])\rVert_{L^1}$ |
     |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
     | $0$ | $\tfrac{3}{8}$ | $\tfrac{1}{3}$ | $\tfrac{1}{2}$ | $\tfrac{1}{4}$ |
     | $1$ | $\tfrac{1}{8}$ | $\tfrac{1}{9}$ | $\tfrac{2}{3}$ | $\tfrac{1}{12}$ |
     | $2$ | $\tfrac{1}{24}$ | $\tfrac{1}{27}$ | $\tfrac{13}{18}$ | $\tfrac{1}{36}$ |
     | $3$ | $\tfrac{1}{72}$ | $\tfrac{1}{81}$ | $\tfrac{20}{27}$ | $\tfrac{1}{108}$ |
     | $4$ | $\tfrac{1}{216}$ | $\tfrac{1}{243}$ | $\tfrac{121}{162}$ | $\tfrac{1}{324}$ |
     | $8$ | $\tfrac{1}{17496}$ | $\tfrac{1}{19683}$ | $\tfrac{9841}{13122}$ | $\tfrac{1}{26244}$ |

**Definitions (`eLpNorm`, `MemLp`, `Lp E p μ`, metric bridges, and controlled Cauchy extraction).**

1. *Extended $L^p$ seminorms and `MemLp` (`LpSeminorm/Defs.lean:69–120`, `EssSup.lean:46–47, 238–241`).* Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be a measure space and $E$ carry an extended norm (`[SeminormedAddGroup E]` or `[NormedAddCommGroup E]`, with $\lVert x\rVert_e = (\lVert x\rVert_+ : \mathbb R_{\ge 0\infty})$). For $q \in \mathbb R$ and $p \in [0, \infty]$ (`ℝ≥0∞`), Mathlib sets
   $$\operatorname{eLpNorm}'(f, q, \mu) := \left(\int^- a,\, \lVert f(a)\rVert_e^q\,d\mu\right)^{1/q}, \qquad \operatorname{eLpNormEssSup}(f, \mu) := \operatorname{essSup}(\lVert f(\cdot)\rVert_e, \mu) = \limsup_{x \sim \mathrm{ae}\,\mu} \lVert f(x)\rVert_e = \inf\bigl\{c \in [0, \infty] : \mu\{x : c < \lVert f(x)\rVert_e\} = 0\bigr\},$$
   and unifies all exponents in `eLpNorm f p μ` (`0` if $p = 0$, `eLpNormEssSup f μ` if $p = \infty$, and `eLpNorm' f p.toReal μ` if $0 < p < \infty$). A function $f : \alpha \to E$ satisfies `MemLp f p μ` iff `AEStronglyMeasurable f μ ∧ eLpNorm f p μ < ∞` (`Defs.lean:118–119`; at $p = 1$, `memLp_one_iff_integrable` identifies `MemLp f 1 μ ↔ Integrable f μ`).
2. *Quotient space `Lp E p μ` and metric/`eLpNorm` equivalences (`LpSpace/Basic.lean:89–112, 217–266, 388–403`, `Complete.lean:110–155`).* For `[NormedAddCommGroup E]`, `Lp E p μ` is the additive subgroup $\{f \in \alpha \to_m[\mu] E : \operatorname{eLpNorm}(f, p, \mu) < \infty\}$ of $\mu$-a.e. equivalence classes of a.e.-strongly measurable maps, with `MemLp.toLp f hf : Lp E p μ` (`hf.toLp f =ᵐ[μ] f`), norm $\lVert f\rVert = (\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f, p, \mu)).\mathrm{toReal}$, and distance $\operatorname{dist}(f, g) = (\operatorname{eLpNorm}(\Uparrow f - \Uparrow g, p, \mu)).\mathrm{toReal}$. Under `[Fact (1 ≤ p)]`, Minkowski's inequality `eLpNorm_add_le` (`TriangleInequality.lean:52–61`) equips `Lp E p μ` with `NormedAddCommGroup (Lp E p μ)`. Because $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(\Uparrow f - \Uparrow g, p, \mu) \neq \infty$ (`Lp.eLpNorm_ne_top`), `ENNReal.tendsto_toReal_iff` identifies metric convergence and Cauchy sequences in `Lp E p μ` with `eLpNorm` convergence to $0 \in \mathbb R_{\ge 0\infty}$:
   - `tendsto_Lp_iff_tendsto_eLpNorm'` (`Complete.lean:110–118`) and `tendsto_Lp_iff_tendsto_eLpNorm` (`:119–128`): for any filter $\mathcal F$ on $\iota$, $f : \iota \to \mathrm{Lp}\,E\,p\,\mu$, and `hf_lim : MemLp f_lim p μ`, $\mathcal F.\mathrm{Tendsto}\,f\,(\mathcal N(hf_{\lim}.\mathrm{toLp}\,f_{\lim})) \iff \mathcal F.\mathrm{Tendsto}\,(\lambda n,\, \operatorname{eLpNorm}(\Uparrow(f_n) - f_{\lim}, p, \mu))\,(\mathcal N(0))$;
   - `cauchySeq_Lp_iff_cauchySeq_eLpNorm` (`:148–154`): $\mathrm{CauchySeq}\,f \iff \mathrm{Tendsto}\,(\lambda (n, m),\, \operatorname{eLpNorm}(\Uparrow(f_n) - \Uparrow(f_m), p, \mu))\,\mathrm{atTop}\,(\mathcal N(0))$.
3. *Controlled Cauchy sequences (`MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean:41–47`, `UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean:838–914`, `Complete.lean:156–190`).* Given a sequence of positive real gauges $B : \mathbb N \to \mathbb R_{> 0}$ (specialized to $B_n = (1/2)^n$ at `Complete.lean:163`), a sequence $u : \mathbb N \to \alpha$ in a pseudometric space is *$B$-controlled* if $\forall N, n, m \in \mathbb N,\ N \le n \to N \le m \to \operatorname{dist}(u_n, u_m) < B_N$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean:110–190`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean#L110-L190) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Basic.lean:89–112,217–266,388–403`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Basic.lean#L388-L403) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean:41–47`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean#L41-L47) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean:838–914`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L838-L914)

---

**Lemmas, main theorem, and boundary proposition.**

**Lemma 2.3.2.7f (lower-semicontinuity of `eLpNorm` under $\mu$-a.e. pointwise convergence for $0 \le p \le \infty$).** Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be any measure space and $E$ be any seminormed additive group (`[SeminormedAddGroup E]`).

1. *Finite positive exponent ($0 < q < \infty$, `eLpNorm'_lim_eq_lintegral_liminf`, `eLpNorm'_lim_le_liminf_eLpNorm'`).* If $f : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$ satisfies $\forall n,\ \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(f_n)\,\mu$ and $f_n(x) \to f_{\lim}(x)$ as $n \to \infty$ for $\mu$-a.e. $x \in \alpha$, then
   $$\operatorname{eLpNorm}'(f_{\lim}, q, \mu) = \left(\int^- a,\, \liminf_{n \to \infty} \lVert f_n(a)\rVert_e^q\,d\mu\right)^{1/q} \le \liminf_{n \to \infty} \operatorname{eLpNorm}'(f_n, q, \mu).$$
2. *Essential-supremum exponent ($p = \infty$, `eLpNorm_exponent_top_lim_eq_essSup_liminf`, `eLpNorm_exponent_top_lim_le_liminf_eLpNorm_exponent_top`).* For any nonempty countable linearly ordered index set $\iota$, $f : \iota \to \alpha \to E$, and $f_{\lim} : \alpha \to E$ (without measurability assumptions on $f_n$) such that $f_n(x) \to f_{\lim}(x)$ along `atTop` for $\mu$-a.e. $x \in \alpha$,
   $$\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_{\lim}, \infty, \mu) = \operatorname{essSup}\!\left(\lambda x,\, \liminf_{n \to \infty} \lVert f_n(x)\rVert_e,\, \mu\right) \le \liminf_{n \to \infty} \operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n, \infty, \mu).$$
3. *Unified sequential and filter bounds (`eLpNorm_lim_le_liminf_eLpNorm`, `eLpNorm_le_of_ae_tendsto`).* For every $p \in [0, \infty]$, if $f : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$ has $\forall n,\ \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(f_n)\,\mu$ and $f_n \to f_{\lim}$ $\mu$-a.e., then $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_{\lim}, p, \mu) \le \liminf_{n \to \infty} \operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n, p, \mu)$. Consequently, for any proper countably generated filter $u$ on $\iota$, if $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_i, p, \mu) \le C$ eventually along $u$, $\forall i,\ \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(f_i)\,\mu$, and $f_i(x) \to g(x)$ along $u$ for $\mu$-a.e. $x$, then $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(g, p, \mu) \le C$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean:27–105`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean#L27-L105) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/EssSup.lean:364–369`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/EssSup.lean#L364-L369) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Order/Filter/ENNReal.lean:236–244`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Order/Filter/ENNReal.lean#L236-L244) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Add.lean:202–229`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Add.lean#L202-L229)

**Lemma 2.3.2.7g (summable-difference sequences: common conull convergence for $1 \le p < \infty$ and $p = \infty$, and globally `StronglyMeasurable` limit construction).** Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be an arbitrary measure space, let $E$ be a complete normed additive commutative group (`[NormedAddCommGroup E] [CompleteSpace E]`), let $1 \le p \le \infty$ in $\mathbb R_{\ge 0\infty}$, let $f : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$ satisfy $\forall n,\ \mathrm{AEStronglyMeasurable}\,(f_n)\,\mu$, and let $B : \mathbb N \to \mathbb R_{\ge 0\infty}$ satisfy $\sum_{i=0}^\infty B_i \neq \infty$ (Lean's unconditional topological sum `∑' i, B i ≠ ∞`) and
$$\forall N, n, m \in \mathbb N,\quad N \le n \to N \le m \to \operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n - f_m, p, \mu) < B_N.$$

1. *Finite-exponent telescoping series ($1 \le p < \infty$, `ae_tendsto_of_cauchy_eLpNorm'`).* Putting $q := p.\mathrm{toReal} \in [1, \infty)$, the infinite series of extended norms satisfies
   $$\left(\int^- a,\, \left(\sum_{i=0}^\infty \lVert f_{i+1}(a) - f_i(a)\rVert_e\right)^q d\mu\right)^{1/q} \le \sum_{i=0}^\infty B_i < \infty.$$
   There is a conull set $\Omega_1 \in \mathrm{ae}\,\mu$ on which $\sum_{i=0}^\infty \lVert f_{i+1}(x) - f_i(x)\rVert_e < \infty$, hence $\sum_{i=0}^\infty (f_{i+1}(x) - f_i(x))$ converges absolutely in $E$ and $f_n(x) \to f_0(x) + \sum_{i=0}^\infty (f_{i+1}(x) - f_i(x))$ in $E$.
2. *Essential-supremum countable conull intersection ($p = \infty$, `ae_tendsto_of_cauchy_eLpNorm`).* There is a single common conull set $\Omega_\infty \in \mathrm{ae}\,\mu$ on which $\lVert f_n(x) - f_m(x)\rVert_e < B_N$ holds simultaneously for all triples $(N, n, m) \in \mathbb N^3$ with $N \le n$ and $N \le m$; at every $x \in \Omega_\infty$, $(f_n(x))_{n \in \mathbb N}$ is Cauchy in $E$ with modulus $(B_N).\mathrm{toReal} \to 0$, hence converges in $E$.
3. *Globally `StronglyMeasurable` limit without global separability or measure completeness (`exists_stronglyMeasurable_limit_of_tendsto_ae`).* In both cases ($1 \le p < \infty$ and $p = \infty$), there exists a **globally `StronglyMeasurable`** function $f_{\lim} : \alpha \to E$ and a single common conull set $\Omega \in \mathrm{ae}\,\mu$ such that $f_n(x) \to f_{\lim}(x)$ in $E$ for every $x \in \Omega$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean:193–320,366–370`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean#L193-L320) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/AEStronglyMeasurable.lean:711–724`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/AEStronglyMeasurable.lean#L711-L724) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Constructions/BorelSpace/Metrizable.lean:106–125`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Constructions/BorelSpace/Metrizable.lean#L106-L125) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Markov.lean:148–155`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Markov.lean#L148-L155)

**Theorem 2.3.2.7h (Riesz–Fischer completeness of `Lp E p μ`, `Lp.instCompleteSpace`).** Let $(\alpha, \mathcal M, \mu)$ be any measure space, let $E$ be any complete normed additive commutative group (`[NormedAddCommGroup E] [CompleteSpace E]`), and let $1 \le p \le \infty$ (`[Fact (1 ≤ p)]`).

1. *$L^p$ norm convergence and `MemLp` / integrability for controlled sequences (`cauchy_tendsto_of_tendsto`, `memLp_of_cauchy_tendsto`, `cauchy_complete_eLpNorm`).* If $f : \mathbb N \to \alpha \to E$ satisfies $\forall n,\ \mathrm{MemLp}\,(f_n)\,p\,\mu$ and $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n - f_m, p, \mu) < B_N$ ($N \le n, m$) with $\sum_{i=0}^\infty B_i \neq \infty$, then the globally `StronglyMeasurable` function $f_{\lim} : \alpha \to E$ of Lemma 2.3.2.7g(3) satisfies
   $$\lim_{n \to \infty} \operatorname{eLpNorm}(f_n - f_{\lim}, p, \mu) = 0 \qquad \text{and} \qquad \mathrm{MemLp}\,f_{\lim}\,p\,\mu$$
   (in particular `Integrable f_lim μ` when $p = 1$).
2. *Subsequence extraction and return to every Cauchy sequence and Cauchy filter (`completeSpace_lp_of_cauchy_complete_eLpNorm`, `Lp.instCompleteSpace`).* The space `Lp E p μ` is complete (`CompleteSpace (Lp E p μ)`): every Cauchy filter $\mathcal F$ on `Lp E p μ` converges, and in particular every Cauchy sequence $(F_k)_{k \in \mathbb N}$ in `Lp E p μ` admits a strictly increasing index sequence $(k_n)_{n \in \mathbb N}$ with $\lVert F_m - F_r\rVert_{L^p} < 2^{-n}$ ($m, r \ge k_n$) and a class $[f_{\lim}] = hf_{\lim}.\mathrm{toLp}\,f_{\lim} \in \mathrm{Lp}\,E\,p\,\mu$ such that $F_{k_n}(x) \to f_{\lim}(x)$ $\mu$-a.e., $\lVert F_{k_n} - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^p} \to 0$ as $n \to \infty$, and $\lVert F_k - [f_{\lim}]\rVert_{L^p} \to 0$ along the **entire** sequence $k \to \infty$. [Lean, read] [Mathlib, `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean:156–190,321–382`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/LpSpace/Complete.lean#L321-L382) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean:41–47,152–160`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Cauchy.lean#L41-L47) · [Mathlib, `Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean:838–914`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L838-L914)

**Proposition 2.3.2.7i (hypothesis boundaries: necessity of `[CompleteSpace E]` and breakdown of the normed argument for $0 < p < 1$).**

1. *Necessity of codomain completeness (`completeSpace_of_completeSpace_Lp`, plus explicit infinite-measure $c_{00}$ witness).* Let $E$ be a normed $\mathbb R$-space and $1 \le p \le \infty$. Whenever `Lp E p μ` is nontrivial (for $E \neq \{0\}$, iff $\mu \neq 0$ when $p = \infty$, and iff there exists $s \in \mathcal M$ with $0 < \mu(s) < \infty$ when $1 \le p < \infty$) and `CompleteSpace (Lp E p μ)` holds, `CompleteSpace E` holds (`CompleteOfCompleteLp.lean:23–121`, `[Lean, read]`). Explicitly, for $E = c_{00}(\mathbb N_{\ge 1})$ (with $\lVert\cdot\rVert_\infty$ or $\lVert\cdot\rVert_1$) on $(\mathbb R_{\ge 0}, \mathcal B(\mathbb R_{\ge 0}), \mathrm{vol})$, the sequence $F_N := \bigl(\sum_{m=1}^N 2^{-m} e_m\bigr) 1_{[0, 1]} \in \mathrm{Lp}\,c_{00}\,p\,\mathrm{vol}$ is Cauchy for every $1 \le p \le \infty$ ($\lVert F_M - F_N\rVert_{L^p} \le 2^{-N}$ for $M > N$) and has **no** limit in `Lp c₀₀ p vol` (`[Expository]`).
2. *Quasi-norm constant `LpAddConst` and telescoping failure for $0 < p < 1$ and completeness for the $p$-metric.* For $0 < p < 1$, `eLpNorm` satisfies the sharp quasi-triangle inequality $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f + g, p, \mu) \le 2^{1/p.\mathrm{toReal} - 1}\bigl(\operatorname{eLpNorm}(f, p, \mu) + \operatorname{eLpNorm}(g, p, \mu)\bigr)$ (`LpAddConst`, `MeanInequalitiesPow.lean:323`, `TriangleInequality.lean:37–75`, `[Lean, read]`), attained on indicators of any two disjoint measurable sets of equal finite positive measure, so $\operatorname{eLpNorm}(\cdot, p, \mu)^{\mathrm{toReal}}$ is not a norm. At $p = \frac{1}{2}$ on $([0, 1), \mathrm{vol})$, enumerating the dyadic intervals $I_{r, j} = \bigl[\frac{j}{2^r}, \frac{j+1}{2^r}\bigr)$ ($r \ge 0$, $0 \le j < 2^r$) as $E_k$ ($k = 2^r + j \ge 1$) and setting $h_k := \sum_{m=1}^k (-1)^m 1_{E_m}$ ($h_0 := 0$) yields
   $$\sum_{k=1}^\infty \operatorname{eLpNorm}'(h_k - h_{k-1}, \tfrac{1}{2}, \mathrm{vol}) = \sum_{r=0}^\infty 2^r \cdot 4^{-r} = 2 < \infty,$$
   whereas $\operatorname{eLpNorm}'\bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{2^{R+1}-1} \lvert h_k - h_{k-1}\rvert, \tfrac{1}{2}, \mathrm{vol}\bigr) = R + 1 \to +\infty$, $(h_k(x))$ diverges at **every** $x \in [0, 1)$, and $\operatorname{eLpNorm}'(h_{2^{r+1}-1} - h_{2^r - 1}, \tfrac{1}{2}, \mathrm{vol}) = 1 \not\to 0$ (`[Expository]`). Replacing $\operatorname{eLpNorm}'$ by the true translation-invariant $p$-metric $d_p(f, g) := \int \lVert f - g\rVert^p\,d\mu = \operatorname{eLpNorm}'(f - g, p, \mu)^p$ and requiring $\sum_{i=0}^\infty d_p(f_{i+1}, f_i) < \infty$ restores completeness of $L^p(\mu; E)$ ($0 < p < 1$, `[CompleteSpace E]`) as a complete metric group. For a Banach vector space $E$ it is an $F$-space, a topological vector space with a complete translation-invariant metric. [Expository].
